| 10.2.2 | Graph repair heuristics | Insert implicit end node, merge duplicate nodes, drop unreachable noise | `src/assemble/graphrepair.py`: three repairs, each returning a log entry `{repair, refs, detail, before, after}` - **nothing is ever fixed silently**, which is what the definition of done actually asks for - and each keyed to a signal 10.2.1 showed is discriminative. **The primary measurement is damage, not benefit**: every repair is run over all **5,796 ground-truth files first, where by construction every edit it makes is a false positive.** `insert_implicit_end` fires on **4.18%**, exactly `FC.END_ONE`'s zero-count rate, since the gate *is* the rule. `merge_duplicate_nodes` fires on **0 of 5,796**, and the IoU sweep explains why rather than leaving it as a clean bill of health: ground truth contains essentially no same-labelled overlapping pair at any threshold - one at 0.3, none at 0.5 and above - so its 0.99 recall / 1.00 precision on injected duplicates is honest but **the corpus never asked for this repair**. **`drop_unreachable_noise` is the row's real story and it re-runs 10.2.1's lesson one level down**: its first cut fired on **553 of 693 hdbpmn pages - 79.8% of correct graphs** - and the cause was not a threshold but two roles that are edge-free *by construction rather than by defect*: `container` (pool and lane frames, edge-free 1,338 of 1,766 times) and `io` (BPMN data objects, edge-free **1,085 of 1,085 - every single one** - because hdBPMN's association arrows are not carried as edges in this IR at all). Exempting both from the component graph drops it to **1.81% (18 of 993)**. Under controlled damage all three recover well - `insert_implicit_end` 1.00/1.00, `merge_duplicate_nodes` 0.99 recall at 1.00 precision, `drop_unreachable_noise` 1.00 recall at **0.9881** precision, the residual being pre-existing anchor-free fragments the exemption does not cover. **The connection back to 10.2.1 is structural and not decorative: in both rows a plausible rule fired on the correct majority of a corpus, and both times the fix was finding which role explained it rather than tuning a threshold** - which is exactly the failure a repair pass must not ship, since a repair keyed on 10.2.1's unqualified decision rule would have rewritten 404 of 693 correct BPMN pages | ✅ |
| 10.2.3 | Cycle / loop detection | Tarjan SCC → mark loops for codegen | `src/assemble/loops.py`: Tarjan written out rather than imported - one DFS, an explicit `(node, position)` stack, a component closed on `low[v] == index[v]` - then turned into something a code generator can consume. Each cyclic component is classified `self_loop` / `simple_cycle` / `complex` and carries its entry nodes, entry/exit/back edges and a nesting depth from a loop-nesting forest; `mark()` writes `loop_id`, `loop_role`, `loop_kind`, `loop_depth` into the IR `attrs` where 10.3 reads them. Over all **5,796 ground-truth files: 24.0% contain a cycle, 2,197 loops, largest SCC 21 nodes, deepest nest 4**. **One loop in every 5.5 is irreducible - 397 of 2,197 (18.1%)** - and it is not one corpus dragging the average: didi 13.4%, hdbpmn 20.4%, fa_bresler 21.3%. Compiler literature treats irreducible control flow as a curiosity; in graphs people drew by hand it is a fifth of all loops, so **10.3 cannot ship a while-only generator and call the remainder an edge case**. **The corpora split hard and pooling them would have hidden it**: fa_bresler has a cycle in 100% of its files and **all 371 of the corpus's self-loops are its**, against 102 simple cycles - a state machine's "stay here on this symbol"; hdbpmn has **zero** self-loops, the 21-node SCC, the depth-4 nest, and 318 of its 646 loops complex; **flowchartseg (1,319 files) and sketch2code (484) contribute zero loops between them** - containment is a tree by construction and the segmented flowcharts are acyclic - so a third of the corpus can never exercise this code. **585 loops (27%) have no exit edge at all**, and that is ground truth rather than detector noise: a quarter of hand-drawn loops are non-terminating as drawn, every one a `while (true)` nobody intended, which argues for a warning rather than trust. Correctness is checked against the definition rather than asserted - brute-force reachability (u reaches v and v reaches u) on **5,497 of 5,796 files, zero disagreements**, plus 200 random graphs in the tests. **The stated reason for the iterative Tarjan turned out to be false and is left in the docstring saying so**: sketch2code's 200-node page is broad, not deep, and the deepest DFS path in the whole corpus is **27 nodes**, so recursion would have survived with a factor of forty to spare - the explicit stack is insurance against the next corpus, not a fix for this one | ✅ |
| 10.2.4 | IR serialization | Emit validated `ir.json` plus Graphviz/Mermaid preview | `src/assemble/serialise.py`: all **5,796** ground-truth IR files round-tripped through the **consumer's loader** rather than through `json` - `Diagram → dict → json → dict → Diagram`, re-validated against `schemas/ir.schema.json` - at a **pass rate of 1.0**. That distinction is the entire point of the row and it is the bar 9.3.7 did not have: its bug wrote `low_conf_text` as bare id strings, which satisfies `json.loads(json.dumps(x)) == x` perfectly and only fails on the *next* `Diagram.from_dict`, three commits away from where it was introduced. It is reproduced here as a dedicated regression test, so the shape of that bug cannot come back unnoticed. **Byte stability is 0.9784 (5,671 of 5,796), and the 125 misses were decomposed rather than asserted**: every one is hdbpmn and every one is `crlf-line-endings` on disk against `Diagram.save`'s `\n`-only writer - a line-ending artefact of how those files were written, not a content change - and every other source is 100% byte-stable. DOT and Mermaid emitters are structurally valid on all 5,796 real diagrams and on an adversarial set of **24 labels × 12 shapes** covering quotes, pre-escaped backslash-quotes, CRLF, bare `-->` and `---`, `#`, control bytes, unicode, emoji, HTML, the empty string and a DOT keyword used as a label: **zero escaping failures in either emitter**, which is the honest surprise, since escaping and not round-tripping was the part expected to break. **The one check that could not be run is reported as not run**: `dot` is not installed on this machine, `shutil.which` returns `None`, and the result records `dot_binary_available: false` with the note that this is a structural check rather than a claim the real parser accepted the output - a fake pass here would have been worse than no check. On scale, node counts run p50 **6**, p90 31, p99 187, max **200**, and **299 diagrams (5.16%) exceed the 60-node preview cap - all 299 of them sketch2code**, whose 200-node wireframe trees are the reason a cap exists at all; every other corpus stays under it, and both emitters truncate and report how many nodes were cut rather than emitting an unreadable preview | ✅ |
| 10.2.5 | IR diff tool | Predicted vs. ground-truth IR (node F1, edge F1, graph edit distance) | `src/assemble/irdiff.py`: the metric every other Phase 10 row is quoted in, so the correspondence problem *is* the task - a predicted node has a different id from the true node it represents, and **node F1 is undefined until the matching rule is named**. Three rules are implemented and compared rather than one being chosen silently: geometric (IoU, Hungarian-optimal via `linear_sum_assignment`, not greedy), text (normalised label agreement), and combined. **The matching rule alone moves node F1 by 0.199** - geometric 0.9005 against text 0.7015, with the worst single graph swinging a full 1.0 - so a node F1 quoted without naming its matcher is a number that can be moved by a fifth of a point at will. **Even self-identity is not free of the choice**: geometric and gated diff a diagram against itself at exactly F1 1.0 and GED 0, while text and combined do not (0.795, 0.9193), because **50.48% of this corpus's nodes carry no text at all** and blank-against-blank is scored as a non-match by design. Edge F1 is then scored on *matched* node pairs, so a single node miss cascades - and finishing that study was the interrupted work: **the original perturbation deleted nodes, which drags their incident edges out with them and hid the very effect being measured, reporting 0.0 lost edges per miss as an artefact**. Re-run with a perturbation that jitters a box past the IoU gate and leaves the edge set intact, **one missed node costs 2.5757 edges** and **59.25% of true edges in a damaged graph become unreachable to any predictor**, which is the honest reason edge F1 trails node F1 by **0.1855** (0.3587 against 0.5442). **Graph edit distance is stated as an approximation and then bounded rather than asserted**: exact GED is NP-hard, so an assignment-based bound is built on the node matching already computed and checked against brute force over all permutations on 825 small-graph pairs (≤7 nodes) - **it never undershoots the exact value, matches it in 99.39% of pairs, and has a mean gap of 0.0412 (3.76% relative)**, worst under box jitter and exactly zero under node deletion, edge deletion and relabelling. Every monotonicity curve across five perturbation kinds moves in the intended direction over k = 0..4, and the only flat lines are rules correctly blind to damage outside their own evidence. An end-to-end figure against a trivial detections-only predictor is on the board at node F1 0.6615, edge F1 **0.0** once restricted to pages that actually have edges | ✅ |
| 10.2.6 | Confidence propagation | Aggregate detection / OCR / HMM confidences into node and edge confidence | `src/assemble/propagate.py`: four aggregation rules - product, minimum, weighted mean, noisy-or - each scored the way 9.3.7 scored its candidates, **by AUROC at predicting that the node is actually wrong, with ECE reported separately, because the ranking and the calibration are different questions**. **The first version of this row was wrong and the correction is recorded rather than quietly overwritten**: it scored all 24,385 raw detections, of which **66.4% are the derived `arrowhead` class** that can never match a ground-truth node, so its 0.6884 "wrong" rate was essentially the arrowhead share and its 0.9955 control AUROC was measuring arrowhead-versus-shape separability rather than confidence quality. Re-scored on **10.1.1's assembled population - 7,729 nodes, 2.08% wrong** - the picture is harder and real: **`noisy_or` reaches AUROC 0.9300 against the free detector-score-alone control at 0.9203, a margin of just +0.0097**, because OCR and HMM annotate only 25% and 61% of nodes and the rule reduces to the bare detector score everywhere else. **Two of the four rules score *below* the control** - product 0.6805 and minimum 0.7382 - and the mechanism is visible in the bins: multiplying or floor-taking punishes a node for any one mediocre signal even when it is otherwise correct, so **product's worst decile has mean confidence 0.2755 and is 97.93% correct**. That is the concrete argument against the obvious implementation, which is to multiply three numbers as if they were independent probabilities. **The calibration result is reported with its own caveat rather than as a win**: `noisy_or` is also best-calibrated here at ECE 0.0203 against the detector's 0.0818, but on a 98%-correct population the bins are flat and tightly clustered, so that is an artefact of the base rate and not evidence the score resolves fine-grained confidence - **it remains a rank and never a percentage**, exactly as 9.3.7 concluded, and Phase 16 must not render it as a probability. **Edges are an honest negative result**: with no independent edge detector upstream, endpoint-confidence stand-ins score **0.547-0.558 AUROC on 8,975 pairs - essentially chance** - so edge confidence needs a signal that does not yet exist. The aggregate is written into `Node.confidence` and `Edge.confidence` and validated through `Diagram.require_valid()` and `Diagram.load` rather than a hand-rolled dict, which is the check 9.3.7's bare-id bug needed and lacked | ✅ |

---

# Phase 11 — Reinforcement Learning Traversal Agent (Unit 4)

**Goal:** learn to traverse ambiguous diagrams in the order that produces correct code.

### 11.1 MDP Formulation

| # | Element | Definition | Definition of Done | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| 11.1.1 | **State** | current node + visited set + detected connections + unresolved-edge flags + node role | `src/rl/state.py` + `tests/test_rl_state.py`: `DiagramGraph.from_ir` compiles a diagram once into tuples and adjacency, `TraversalState` is a frozen hashable record carrying visited/emitted/loop-marked as **bitmasks** so a state is cheap to hash into a Q-table, and `StateEncoder.features` returns a **constant width of 28**, every entry in [0, 1]. Measured over **3,993 diagrams / 24,503 nodes**: **530 unresolved edges (13.27%)** and **1,389 back edges (34.79%)**, which is why unresolved-edge flags and loop marks are in the state at all rather than assumed away. **A bug in `roots()` was found by the inherited docstring disagreeing with itself** - the class said width 27 and the module said 28 - and chasing that turned up the real defect: `roots()` counted a **self-loop as an incoming edge**, so a node whose only predecessor was itself was never an entry point and its component could not be entered. It cost coverage on **811 single-component diagrams**. The inherited node count (22,503) was stale as well and is corrected to 24,503 | ✅ |
| 11.1.2 | **Actions** | follow-edge-A, follow-edge-B, …, backtrack, mark-as-loop, emit-node, terminate | `src/rl/actions.py` + `tests/test_rl_actions.py`: **9 flat actions** - five `follow-edge` slots plus `backtrack`, `mark-as-loop`, `emit-node`, `terminate` - with a hard `action_mask` and `legal_actions` that is never empty because `terminate` is always legal. **The branching factor was measured rather than assumed and the inherited constant was fiction**: `MAX_BRANCH` was 10, justified by a histogram running to a 10-way split, and **no such diagram exists in the corpus**. The real out-degree distribution is `{1: 1772, 2: 1546, 3: 554, 4: 88, 5: 33}` - **maximum 5** - so the action space is 9 rather than 14, and every one of the five follow slots is reachable by some real diagram. Over 28,968 steps the mask leaves **3.29 legal actions per step**, so the space is small enough for the tabular methods 11.2.1 needs. A crash in the module's own `measure()` helper was fixed on the way | ✅ |
| 11.1.3 | **Reward** | +1 syntactically valid code; bonus for semantic correctness (code runs and passes tests); penalty for infinite loops, unreachable nodes, duplicate emission | `src/rl/reward.py` + `src/rl/emit.py` + `tests/test_rl_reward.py`: a four-term semantic score (coverage 0.40, ordering 0.25, structure 0.20, loops 0.15) plus step-level penalties, with 11.2.9's sandbox wired in for the execution bonus. **The inherited reward was anti-correlated with traversal quality and would have taught 11.2 to traverse backwards** - it scored a *reversed* traversal above the gold one (structure 0.9997 against 0.8915). The cause is exact: `emit.py` emitted `_cond`/`_again` predicates and deliberately left them undefined, so `undefined_names` was counting **structure the policy had successfully built**, and gold scored 89.83% undefined against reversed's 0.83% - the inherited docstring claimed precisely the opposite figures. Fixed by defining every predicate and adding an `EDGES` table naming both endpoints, so an undefined name now means only *referenced a node it never emitted*, and by adding `order_score`, since coverage is structurally blind to ordering. The ranking is now monotone and independently re-checked: **gold +2.85 > reversed +2.01 > half +1.70 > random +0.50** with coverage held fixed. **Two limitations are recorded rather than hidden**: Phase 12 does not exist, so semantic correctness is measured against a template emitter and not an LLM; and although the sandbox is genuinely wired (71.4 ms per call, terminal-only, 61x the static proxy), gold passes 126/200 - **exactly the 63.06% coverage ceiling** - so on this emitter the sandbox verdict is close to a coverage indicator rather than an independent semantic measure. **`src/codegen/targets.py` was tried first and rejected on measurement**: it reports an undefined name on 100% of orders, gold and reversed alike, giving zero discrimination, and has no parameter for `mark-as-loop`, so the reward could not price an action 11.1.2 requires | ✅ |
| 11.1.4 | Episode definition | One diagram = one episode; terminal on full coverage or step cap | `src/rl/episode.py` + `tests/test_rl_episode.py`: the transition function, three terminal conditions (`coverage` / `policy` / `cap`) and a step cap of **4n + 8**. Measured on the gold traversal over 3,993 diagrams, episode length is **p50 5, p95 26, max 65**, and **0 of 3,993 gold episodes hit the cap** - the cap exists to stop a policy looping forever, and a cap that truncated the *reference* behaviour would be measuring itself. **Two inherited claims were false and are corrected rather than adjusted**: it stated 100% gold full coverage, and the real figure is **63.06%**, because the action space has no jump and a traversal cannot cross into a second connected component - so **63.06% is the coverage ceiling any policy in this action space can reach, and 11.2's numbers must be read against it rather than as policy failure**. It also claimed the cap truncates 10.60% of random episodes; it truncates **none** - a random policy stops early instead, reaching 2.68% coverage and terminating by its own choice 97.32% of the time, with 0 episodes that never stop | ✅ |
| 11.1.5 | Gym environment | `DiagramTraversalEnv` with `reset` / `step` / `render` | `src/rl/env.py` + `tests/test_rl_env.py`: `reset(seed, options) -> (obs, info)` and `step(action) -> (obs, reward, terminated, truncated, info)` in the standard five-tuple shape, with `Discrete(9)` and `Box(0, 1, (28,))` written natively - **`gymnasium` and `gym` were both probed for and are absent**, so depending on either would have been an import error rather than a design choice, and that is recorded instead of a silent requirements addition. `api_check` is a real gate rather than a smoke test: **68,154 assertions over 400 episodes and 7,306 steps with 0 failures**, covering the tuple shapes, observation bounds, seeded determinism, action-mask correctness, and that stepping a finished episode raises rather than continuing silently. Robustness is the number that matters for a corpus this messy - **0 crashes across all 3,993 diagrams**, including cycles, disconnected components, unresolved edges with `None` endpoints and garbled OCR text - at **21,781 steps/s**, so 11.2's tabular training is not environment-bound. **The inherited docstring's throughput claim was not reproducible**: it reported 1,600 episodes and 24,881 steps, where re-running its own driver produced **118 steps**, because that driver fires `TERMINATE` immediately | ✅ |
| 11.1.6 | State abstraction | Hashing / discretization to keep the Q-table tractable | `src/rl/abstraction.py` + `tests/test_rl_abstraction.py`: a **9-factor product key** (role, out-degree, unvisited successors, visited fraction, emitted fraction, current-emitted, stack depth, open loop, unresolved) with factor sizes `(10, 4, 4, 5, 5, 2, 4, 2, 2)`, so the bound of **128,000** holds by construction rather than by hope, and `key_index` is a proven bijection into `[0, BOUND)`. The bound is the guarantee; the measurement is that only **1,766 distinct keys (1.38%) are actually reached** over the corpus, and a Q-table at the full bound is **9.22 MB** - comfortably tabular, which is what 11.2.1 needs. Against the naive encoding the compression is real but modest where it matters: the naive state space bound is **5.3e37** while only **10,876** naive keys are reached, so the honest figure is **6.16x** on reached states, not the astronomical ratio the bound suggests. **The inherited bound was wrong at 140,800** because it assumed an 11-way role factor where the vocabulary has 10, and its claim that no two raw states share a key was false - **2,738 (25.2%) do**, which is what an abstraction is for and is now stated instead of denied. Three alternatives re-measured and rejected: hashing into a fixed table (1.47% collisions for no bound guarantee), keeping raw counts instead of fractions (1.41x more reached states), and an exact stack (5.30x, and unbounded in principle) | ✅ |

### 11.2 Learning

| # | Task | Detail | Definition of Done | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| 11.2.1 | **Q-learning** | Tabular; α / γ sweep; ε-greedy with decay | `src/rl/qlearning.py` + `tests/test_rl_qlearning.py` + `reports/rl_qlearning.md`: a tabular agent over 11.1.6's bounded key, a 16-cell α × γ sweep, geometric ε decay 1.0 → 0.05, and the shared training loop 11.2.2-11.2.6 reuse. **The Q-table does not converge, and this row says so rather than reporting the last epoch as a result.** \|TD\| over the final 10% of training is **0.8224** of its opening value at 60,000 episodes and **0.8186** at 250,000 - a 4x budget moves it by 0.004 - and greedy performance *falls* over that budget (full coverage 7.35% → 1.11%, edge F1 0.2729 → 0.2201). It is a plateau, not under-training. **What it does learn is real but small**: on the 993 labelled diagrams it beats a random policy on every measure (terminal reward **−2.5545 vs −4.2598**, edge F1 **0.2729 vs 0.2162**) and clears the **0.2033** empty-policy floor - the score an agent that emits nothing already gets from the document-order tail - reaching **17.63% of gold's coverage** (7.35% against gold's 41.69%). On 11.2.7's ambiguous set it never reaches full coverage once and its 0.1855 edge F1 sits just above the 0.1479 floor. **It loses to the baselines and the row reports the loss twice, because only one of the two comparisons is fair**: against the `dfs` arm it is 0.2729 to 0.5849, but that arm is a whole-graph permutation that walks into a second connected component *because it never passes through the action space*; holding the action space fixed, **gold play inside it scores 0.3648 edge F1 and 41.69% coverage**, so 0.3648 - not 0.5849 - is the real ceiling here and the learning gap is 0.09 F1 and 34 points of coverage. The likely cause is recorded as a hypothesis for 11.2.5 to test, not as a conclusion: 11.1.6 measured that 25.2% of raw states share an abstract key, so the policy is being learned over a state that cannot separate the situations it needs to. The sweep is ranked on terminal reward, never on edge F1 - ranking on the transfer metric would be selecting on the test - and moves the result by 1.19 reward across the whole grid against a 2.5 gap to gold, so the hyper-parameters are not what is limiting it. **Two implementation choices are load-bearing and tested**: the bootstrap max is taken over the *legal* actions only (`terminate` is legal everywhere, so an unmasked max leaks the value of an illegal follow-edge backwards through the majority of states), and the permutation an arm hands 11.2.7 is completed with **IR document order** after `gold_order` was tried and rejected on measurement - it is 7.3.3's DFS, so an agent that emitted nothing inherited an edge F1 of 0.60 and the table scored the filler rather than the policy | ✅ |
| 11.2.2 | SARSA comparison | On-policy vs. off-policy behaviour on ambiguous diagrams | `src/rl/sarsa.py` + `tests/test_rl_sarsa.py` + `reports/rl_sarsa.md` + `reports/figures/p11_sarsa.png` (comparison plot): both algorithms through 11.2.1's one loop, differing only in the bootstrap (legal max vs. the action actually chosen next, pinned by a hand-worked single-transition test), plus the process-pool `run_jobs` 11.2.3/11.2.4/11.2.8 reuse. The hypothesis was fixed before the run: the cliff here is the −2.0 step-cap truncation, so on-policy SARSA should truncate less and gain most when played with its exploration on. Each algorithm was swept on greedy terminal reward (never edge F1) and picked a different cell - **Q α 0.4 γ 1.0, SARSA α 0.2 γ 0.99** - so both were re-run at *both* cells, **5 seeds × 60,000 episodes each**, evaluated greedily and at ε 0.05 on the 993 labelled and 684 ambiguous diagrams. **The caution is real and survives the cell control**: SARSA hits the cap in **1.90% vs 4.89%** and **1.79% vs 2.44%** of late training episodes (seed ranges disjoint at both cells), its greedy policy truncates 10.17% vs 16.01% at α 0.4 γ 1.0, and its reward sd is 0.08-0.11 against Q-learning's 0.58. **It does not buy reward, and the textbook signature is not reproduced**: at a shared cell SARSA ties (−3.03 vs −3.00, 0.13 s.e.) or **loses by 0.21** (−2.98 vs **−2.78 ± 0.10**, 3.6 s.e.), where its greedy truncation edge also vanishes (9.51% vs 9.29%); played online it still loses (−2.97 vs −2.79), and Q-learning reaches full coverage more often at every cell. The best learner on the ambiguous set is Q-learning at α 0.2 γ 0.99, **−5.03 reward / 0.1825 edge F1, against gold play in the action space at −3.89 / 0.2944** and the 0.1479 empty-policy floor, so neither algorithm closes the 11.2.1 gap. **11.2.1's headline is a single seed and is not representative of its cell**: seed 0 reproduces −2.5545 / 0.2729 exactly, but the 5-seed mean at α 0.4 γ 1.0 is **−2.9952 ± 0.5782 / 0.2357** (seed 0 is best of five), and α 0.2 γ 0.99 is better on the mean and 6x more repeatable - 11.2.5/11.2.6 should start there. **The inherited draft was replaced, not trusted**: it ran both algorithms at α 0.2 γ 0.95 (neither's tuned cell), did not control for the cell, and drew reward (≈ −3) and three 0-1 shares on one bar axis. `qlearning.train` gained one backwards-compatible history key, `truncated` | ✅ |
| 11.2.3 | Exploration study | ε-greedy vs. softmax vs. UCB | `src/rl/exploration.py` + `tests/test_rl_exploration.py` + `reports/rl_exploration.md` + `reports/figures/p11_exploration.png` (reward curves): the three legal-by-construction rules behind `TabularAgent.select`, a sweep of each rule's own parameter ranked on greedy terminal reward (ε floor 0.01, softmax T 0.25, UCB c 2.0 won), then **5 seeds × 60,000 episodes per rule** at 11.2.1's cell, with a **greedy probe every 5,000 episodes** - because training return is the exploring behaviour policy's return and would have ranked the rules wrongly: softmax's training curve sits *above* ε-greedy's for ~35,000 episodes while its greedy policy is the worst of the three. Result on the 993 labelled diagrams: **ε-greedy −2.6028 ± 0.1226 / edge F1 0.2546**, UCB −2.6760 ± 0.1673 / 0.2269 (a tie on reward, 0.8 s.e.), softmax **−3.5728 ± 0.2026** with its greedy policy truncated at the step cap on **32%** of diagrams; on the 684 ambiguous, ε-greedy −4.85 / 0.1944, UCB −4.94 / 0.1814, softmax −5.54 / 0.1811. **UCB learns fastest** - 0.89 reward ahead of ε-greedy at 5,000 episodes (−3.11 vs −4.00) and ahead until ~45,000 - and reaches the most keys (1,678). **No rule changes the 11.2.1 picture**: the best reaches 2.90% full coverage against gold's 41.69% and 0.2546 edge F1 against the 0.3648 in-action-space ceiling; exploration moves greedy reward by ~1.0, the gap to gold is still 1.17. **The ε floor is the finding to carry forward**: 0.01 beats 11.2.1's 0.05 at the same cell (−2.60 ± 0.12 vs −3.00 ± 0.58) with ~5x less seed spread, and because it sat on the grid edge the edge was measured - floor 0.0 gives −2.96 ± 0.04 over 5 seeds - so 0.01 is interior. **Limit stated**: ε-greedy anneals while softmax/UCB use fixed parameters as defined, so "softmax loses" is about fixed-temperature softmax (TD-error tail/head 0.96 vs 0.78). **Inherited draft corrected**: its docstring said each rule was judged at its best swept value, but `study` ran the hard-coded T 1.0 / c 1.0 (−3.788 / −3.191 in the sweep); it used α 0.2 γ 0.95 rather than 11.2.1's cell, trained a separate agent on the ambiguous set, and plotted only training curves; its prediction that aliasing would break UCB is removed because UCB ties the best rule | ✅ |
| 11.2.4 | Reward shaping | Intermediate rewards for valid partial structure; check for unintended exploits | `src/rl/shaping.py` + `tests/test_rl_shaping.py` + `reports/rl_shaping.md` + `reports/figures/p11_shaping.png`: three potential-based terms (emitted share, visited share, and 11.1.3's `structure_score` of the partial emission - "valid partial structure" literally) and three unconstrained +0.25 bonuses, each **trained under its own reward and scored greedily under the unshaped `RewardConfig()`**, 5 seeds × 60,000 episodes at 11.2.2's stable cell (α 0.2 γ 0.99), whose unshaped control reproduces 11.2.2 exactly (−2.7773 ± 0.0985). Verdicts use 2 s.e. of the seed-mean difference: *justified* = unshaped reward above control, *exploit* = shaped training return up while unshaped reward falls. **The exploit is real and measured**: the visit bonus (paid on every follow, revisits included) has the best shaped return in the study (−1.85, +1.16 over control) and the worst real reward, **−4.53, below a uniform random policy (−4.26)** - 1.44 follows per node against gold's 0.40, 25.8% of steps onto visited nodes, 43% of greedy episodes truncated at the cap, and edge F1 on the ambiguous set of exactly the 0.1479 empty-policy floor. **Shaping is justified**: the emitted-share potential gains **+0.37** (2 s.e. 0.14), full coverage 2.76% → 12.93% and edge F1 0.2422 → **0.3178** against the 0.3648 in-action-space gold ceiling (unused for selection), and the structure potential +0.23 with the most coverage (13.35%); both roughly double greedy cap truncation (15-17% vs 9.3%) and still come out ahead. The bounded emit bonus ties it on the 993 (−2.42) and is the best variant on the 684 ambiguous (−4.79 vs −5.03, beyond 2 s.e.). **Policy invariance did not hold in practice and is stated rather than cited**: the visited potential *loses* 0.34 with 31% truncation, because invariance speaks to the optimal policy not to a finite ε-greedy run, and Φ is over raw states while the table indexes 11.1.6's key that aliases 25.2% of them; a test now checks the loop itself pays exactly −Φ(s₀) per episode. Recommendation carried forward: the emitted-share potential. **A first run at 11.2.1's cell was kept, not hidden**: control sd 0.72 over 3 seeds cleared nothing but agreed in direction (visit bonus −1.62, flagged), which is why the study moved to the stable cell. **The inherited draft had the exploit backwards**: it named `loop_bonus` the exploit and `visit_bonus` "also bounded" - the mask bounds marks (measured: 2.1x control's marks, no reward effect) while follows are unbounded - and its exploit rule (marks > 2x gold) could never have caught the one that exists | ✅ |
| 11.2.5 | DQN extension | Function approximation for large graphs where tabular fails | `src/rl/dqn.py` + `tests/test_rl_dqn.py` + `reports/rl_dqn.md` + `reports/figures/p11_dqn.png`: a Double DQN in plain torch over `StateEncoder.features` with masked argmax and masked bootstrap, truncation terminal (as 11.2.1), 11.2.1's reward, ε schedule, 60,000-episode budget and `play` harness. **11.2.1's hypothesis was tested before training and is wrong**: its "25.2% of raw states share a key" is 11.1.6's count of raw keys shared *across diagrams*, not aliasing. Measured over the 10,983 states gold play visits, only **2.99%** sit on an abstract key whose gold actions disagree (0.00% for the 28-vector), and the majority-gold-action table over that key plays to **0.3630 edge F1 against gold's 0.3648** (the features MLP matches gold exactly, 0.3801 = gold on a 198-diagram hold-out) - the table can represent the policy; the tabular plateau is a learning failure. **As a learner the DQN wins, over 3 seeds**: greedy reward **−1.534 ± 0.056** vs 11.2.1's cell −2.942 ± 0.717 and the best combined 11.2.2/11.2.3 table (α 0.2, γ 0.99, ε-floor 0.01) −2.694 ± 0.067, against gold −1.4365 - 93% of the tabular-to-gold gap; edge F1 **0.3411 ± 0.0097** vs 0.2460 / 0.2797 against the 0.3648 in-action-space ceiling and 0.2033 floor; full coverage 28.87% vs 4.70% (gold 41.69%); ambiguous 684 F1 0.2603 vs 0.1718 / 0.2097 (gold 0.2944); \|TD\| tail/head 0.37 vs 0.82. It still loses to the `dfs` arm (0.5849), which no policy in this action space can reach. **Out of sample the claim narrows**: trained on 795 and scored on 198 unseen, reward −1.562 ± 0.110 vs −2.545 ± 0.039 (resolved) but edge F1 0.3262 ± 0.0292 vs tabular_best 0.3027 - inside one DQN SD - against gold 0.3801. By size ("large graphs") the gain is in 6-12 nodes (F1 0.5249 vs 0.3569); on >25 nodes every policy including gold sits near the floor (DQN 0.0921, gold 0.1099) because those pages are multi-component, so 11.1.4's reachability ceiling, not the learner, binds there. Sweep: 8 cells (lr × γ × input with/without legal mask) as one ensemble, ranked on greedy reward, spread −1.635 to −2.134, every cell above every table on record; the mask input bought nothing and was rejected. **GPU profiled before scaling**: eager updates cost 9.5 ms regardless of batch (kernel launches), so the update is one captured CUDA graph (7.43 → 1.25 ms, 3 members, batch 256), members are stacked `(K,out,in)` weights with a per-member Adam and clip tested equal to `torch.optim.Adam`, terminal rewards memoised (77% hits); transitions/s flattens at 64 envs (1 member 1,948; 8 members 5,689, 5,347 at 128), batch 1024 was faster only by taking 4x fewer gradient steps and was rejected; three single-member processes ran 10.2k transitions/s vs 6,961 for one 3-member ensemble (+1,077 vs +499 MiB), so the sweep ran stacked and seeds as processes, all under 3 GB, env CPU 78% of sweep wall time. **Two bugs found by measurement and fixed**: the first capture silently dropped the first update and diverged from eager (now bit-identical, tested), and a missing synchronise before the side-stream warm-up crashed 2 of 6 concurrent runs with an illegal memory access. | ✅ |
| 11.2.6 | Curriculum | Clean synthetic graphs → messy real graphs | `src/rl/curriculum.py` + `tests/test_rl_curriculum.py` + `reports/rl_curriculum.md` + `reports/figures/p11_curriculum.png` (tabular) + `p11_curriculum_dqn.png` (DQN): four stages - synthetic linear (400 pages), synthetic branching/looping/nested (1,096), real single-component ≤12 nodes (422), all 993 real - at 15/20/25/40% of a fixed 60,000-episode budget, five arms (flat, **flat_restarts**, curriculum, reversed, synthetic_only) run for **both learners** (11.2.1's table, 5 seeds; 11.2.5's DQN, 3 seeds), all scored greedily on the 993 labelled diagrams, verdicts paired over seeds on terminal reward with a 2-s.e. band. **The curriculum does not help either learner**: curriculum − flat is −0.069 (2 s.e. 0.419) for the table and −0.011 (0.173) for the DQN; against the schedule-matched control +0.122 (0.228) and +0.117 (0.149), unresolved; edge F1 moves −0.013 and +0.003. **Stage order matters only destructively, and only for the DQN**: ending on the easiest synthetic pages (`reversed`) collapses it to −4.083 ± 0.183, at the random policy's −4.26 (curriculum − reversed +2.299, 2 s.e. 0.155) - the network forgets real pages - while the table does not forget (−0.002 ± 0.163). **The one positive finding is transfer**: the DQN trained on synthetic IR alone reaches 25.08% full coverage and 0.2786 edge F1 on real pages it never saw, above the tabular agent trained on the real pages (0.2357 ± 0.0338), while the synthetic-only table reaches 0.2193 and −4.046 (resolved −1.05 below flat) - the 28-feature state generalises where the abstract key does not. **Inherited draft, measured and corrected**: its own generator produced 96 synthetic pages holding **10 distinct graphs** (24 nested pages were one graph, `index` changed only the id) with **31.58% of nodes encoded `unknown`** (role `terminator` is unmapped) against 8.96% on real pages, so it was deleted for `src.synth.graphs` (unchanged; clean filter single-component + no unresolved edge + gold full coverage, dropping 104 of 1,200; its `io` role still leaves 15.7-16.2% unknown, stated); its claim that `src/synth` "builds images" was false; it compared a curriculum that restarts ε at every stage with a single-decay flat run, so `flat_restarts` was added; `int()` budget slicing could drop episodes and is now exact (tested). **A caveat carried back to 11.2.5**: the DQN `flat` arm is 11.2.5's configuration but scored −1.773 ± 0.179 against 11.2.5's −1.534 ± 0.056, differing only in the replay-sampling stream (ensemble vs separate processes), so 11.2.5's 3-seed SD understates run-to-run spread; both remain far above the best table (−2.69). Tabular flat reproduces the 5-seed −2.9952 ± 0.578 at 11.2.1's cell. | ✅ |
| 11.2.7 | Baseline comparison | Learned policy vs. plain topological sort vs. DFS/BFS | `src/rl/baselines.py` + `tests/test_rl_baselines.py` + `reports/rl_baselines.md`: the four non-learned arms, the definition of the ambiguous set, and the harness that turns them into a win-rate table. **The ambiguous set had to be defined before it could be used, and the first result was that a binary definition is useless**: branch point 93.7%, unresolved edge 56.2%, cyclic 63.5%, multi-component 55.9%, **any of them 993 of 993** - *every* labelled diagram is order-ambiguous, minimum measured ordering freedom 1.0 bit. The set is therefore defined by magnitude, **≥ 4 bits (≥ 16 consistent orders) = 684 of 993 (68.9%)**, 610 hdbpmn / 74 fa_bresler. On that set: **dfs 0.6247 mean edge F1 / 345 outright wins / 50.4%**, topological 0.5466 / 44 / 6.4%, bfs 0.3485 / 1 / 0.1%, and the blind `reading` control 0.1743 / 0 / 0.0% - the control is reported because had it come close the metric would have been scoring page layout rather than flow. **The number a learned arm must beat is 93.4%, not 50.4%**: 43% of the set (294 of 684) has no outright winner and DFS is in the top group on 639 of 684. The 4-bit threshold is not load-bearing - dfs > topological > bfs > reading at every threshold from 1 to 16 bits. Scoring is 10.2.5's `irdiff.diff` edge F1 of the chain the order asserts against the diagram's real edges, with identical denominators across arms; Kendall tau against a reference was rejected as circular (the only candidate reference is 7.3.3's DFS, which hands the dfs arm 1.0) and node F1 as constant 1.0 by construction. **The blocking bug is fixed**: `compare` validated arms against the module's own 5-node `_PROBE` instead of a diagram from the set being run, so `test_a_registered_arm_joins_the_table_and_can_win` failed and **no learned policy could ever have entered the table**; `check_arm` now takes the diagram to probe on. The learned arm joins through `register_arm` and its numbers are in 11.2.1 / 11.2.10 | ✅ |
| 11.2.8 | Convergence diagnostics | Episode reward, TD-error, Q-value heatmaps | `src/rl/diagnostics.py` + `tests/test_rl_diagnostics.py` + `reports/rl_diagnostics.md` + `reports/figures/p11_convergence.png` + `p11_qvalues.png`: episode reward, TD error, greedy-probe reward/coverage/truncation, policy churn, visit concentration and a decoded-key Q heatmap, for **3 seeds × 120,000 episodes** at 11.2.1's cell, reproduced exactly on a second run. **The table has not converged**: 13.5-15.0% of reached keys still flip their greedy action between snapshots 5,000 episodes apart, churn bottoms at ~11% near 50,000 and *rises* again as ε decays, and late greedy probes swing by up to 0.9 reward (seed 0: −3.23 → −3.65 → −4.12 → −3.22 → −2.82). **TD error is the wrong instrument, and a new measurement shows why**: the TD tail/head plateau is 0.79-0.82 (11.2.1's figure), but a *frozen-table floor* - the final table at α 0, which cannot learn - is 0.71-1.10, and training TD sits at 0.84-1.33x that floor, so the residual is target noise (random diagrams, exploration, 11.1.6's 25.2% aliasing), not unfinished learning. **The greedy policy loops before it stops looping**: 45% of greedy episodes hit the step cap mid-training and 18-23% still do at the end, while the exploring training policy never exceeds ~7% - exploration hides the loops, which is why training return (rising +0.011/1k) is a poor proxy here. Final greedy reward −2.90 over seeds, no better than 11.2.2's 60,000-episode −2.9952 ± 0.5782, full coverage never above 6.75% against gold's 41.69%; **11.2.1's single-seed claim that greedy quality falls with budget is not reproduced** at 120,000. Visits: ~1,850 keys reached (1.4% of the bound), half of ~870,000 updates on 53-56 keys, 587-657 keys under 10 updates. The heatmap's most-visited key (5% of updates) is the entry of a disconnected page where emit (−7.23) and terminate (−11.89) are both deep negatives - 11.1.4's ceiling visible in the table. **Inherited draft corrected**: churn used an unmasked argmax that picks a never-taken 0.0-valued action on 5.0-5.8% of keys (measured to move churn by ≤ 0.002 - real, negligible, fixed); the heatmap painted never-updated cells the same white as learned zeros (now grey); its `converged` threshold (TD ratio < 0.25) is unreachable given the noise floor and was removed; the committed figures appear to come from a 2,000-episode run and are replaced | ✅ |
| 11.2.9 | Sandbox execution harness | Safe subprocess with timeout / memory cap to run generated code for the semantic reward | `src/rl/sandbox.py`: `run(code, timeout_s, memory_limit_mb, ...) -> {ok, kind, ...}` with `kind` in `ok/timeout/memory/exception/syntax_error/nonzero_exit/crash/refused`, so the reward keys off one field and **never sees an exception raised by the code it is scoring**. One **Windows job object per call** (`PROCESS_MEMORY \| JOB_MEMORY \| ACTIVE_PROCESS \| KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`) provides both the tree-kill and the memory cap. **The cap is a real hard cap and it was verified against the number, not asserted**: 400x1MB under a 128 MiB limit raises `MemoryError` in the child at `PeakJobMemoryUsed` **134.5 MB (128.3 MiB)**, 0.3% over, which is kernel accounting granularity rather than enforcement slack. **The tree-kill was verified against its counter-example**: a control calling plain `Popen.kill()` on the same parent/grandchild pair leaves the grandchild measurably alive, while the job object clears it in **13.5 ms mean, 20.2 ms max** past the deadline. The spawn race is closed by a bootstrap child that blocks on `stdin.read()` so the job is assigned *before* the program text arrives - otherwise an allocation runs uncapped in the gap. Output is drained by threads that keep reading past their keep-cap, so 64 MB of `print` truncates in 94 ms instead of filling the 64 KB pipe and deadlocking. Overhead is **71.2 ms median** per call (n=50), and a static pre-scan refuses denied imports in **0.005 ms** without spawning. 35 adversarial tests pass, including SIGINT-ignoring loops, unbounded recursion, fork-bombs capped at `ActiveProcessLimit`, flood-plus-timeout, and a byte-identical repo listing before and after code that tries to write into it. **Rejected on the measurement**: `resource.setrlimit` is POSIX-only and would have been the cap that silently does nothing (kept for the POSIX branch and marked unmeasured); a psutil RSS-polling watchdog is advisory by construction, since it observes allocations that already succeeded and `bytearray(1 GB)` commits in full before any sample sees it; a warm interpreter pool would amortise the 71 ms but carries state between episodes, so the reward would stop being a function of the code alone. **The honest limit is recorded in the docstring verbatim: this is a resource sandbox, not a security boundary** - same user, same token, full filesystem access outside its temp cwd, no network isolation, and the import blocklist is a 5 us tripwire for emitter bugs that `__import__("socket")` walks past, with a test asserting exactly that so it cannot later be mistaken for a control | ✅ |
| 11.2.10 | Ablation | Pipeline quality without RL (heuristic ordering only) | `src/rl/ablation.py` + `tests/test_rl_ablation.py` + `reports/rl_ablation.md` + `reports/figures/p11_ablation.png`: every ordering arm is pushed through 11.1.3's `emit_code` and scored end to end - 11.1.3's semantic score, terminal reward, 11.2.7 edge F1, undefined names and 11.2.9's sandbox on a seeded 150-diagram sample (101 hdbpmn / 49 fa_bresler) - over 993 labelled / 684 ambiguous diagrams, 3 seeds, with 11.2.5's DQN checkpoints and 11.2.4's shaped table. **RL as the orderer loses, and the loss is the action space rather than the learning**: DQN minus the best heuristic is **-0.293 ± 0.017 semantic** and **-0.691 sandbox pass** (0.309 vs 1.000), but gold play in the same action space scores 0.590 / 0.473 and DQN sits **-0.004 ± 0.017** from it - a policy that cannot cross components leaves undefined names in 50-67% of programs however well it learns. **On top of DFS the contribution is small and is entirely the loop marks**: `dqn+dfs - dfs` **+0.026 ± 0.013 semantic** (seed 0 paired +0.027, 2 s.e. 0.004, 282 wins / 615 ties / 96 losses), loops 0.314 -> 0.504, and with oracle loop marks for every arm the gain vanishes (**-0.0025 ± 0.007**) - the learned *order* adds nothing. **The order costs edge F1**: -0.024 ± 0.014 on the 993 and -0.050 ± 0.019 on the ambiguous 684 (seed 0: 2 wins / 121 losses); even a perfect prefix (`gold+dfs`) is worth +0.0094 F1 on the 993 and exactly 0 on the ambiguous set. Every full-coverage arm passes the sandbox 150/150. **Contribution quantified: removing RL loses nothing in executability and at most 0.026 semantic score, all from loop marking** - order heuristically (DFS) and keep RL, if at all, for the loop decision. **Inherited draft corrected** (docstring): the sandbox sample was the first 120 diagrams (all hdbpmn), `contribution` counted gold as a heuristic, the learner was a private 11.2.1 table rather than a reported policy, and its "gold loop marks" mode had none (`gold_actions` never marks: 0 of 993). The docstring promised `reports/rl_ablation.md` but nothing wrote it; it is written from `experiments/rl/ablation.json`. DQN checkpoints regenerated 2026-09-12 at 11.2.5's default cell after a lost `experiments/` | ✅ |

# Phase 12 — LLM Fine-Tuning for Code Synthesis (Unit 4)

**Goal:** IR + traversal order + diagram type → clean, runnable code.

### 12.1 Data

| # | Task | Detail | Definition of Done | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| 12.1.1 | Pair schema | `(diagram_type, IR-graph serialization, traversal order) → target code` | `src/codegen/schema.py` + `src/codegen/pairs.py` + `tests/test_codegen_schema.py` (36 tests): the pair is frozen at `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` as **11 keys in fixed order** - `diagram_id, diagram_type, ir_text, traversal, target_code, language, source, split, split_basis, scribe, meta` - one JSON line each; the model input is `(diagram_type, ir_text)` with the traversal as `ir_text`'s `O` line, and `traversal` is stored as a list that `validate` requires to equal that line. **Consumer contract**: `python -m src.codegen.pairs build && python -m src.codegen.pairs merge` writes `data/processed/codegen/pairs/{train,validation,test}.jsonl` (+ per-source shards and `manifest.json` with sha256), and `src.codegen.pairs.load_pairs(split, sources=None, diagram_types=None)` streams validated records; measured real pairs are **train 4,139 (didi 3,000, sketch2code 484, hdbpmn 451, fa_bresler 204) / validation 176 (hdbpmn 128, fa_bresler 48) / test 162 (hdbpmn 114, fa_bresler 48)**, 4,477 total, **0 invalid**, 0 scribe or diagram leaks by 12.1.8's `summarize`, built in 8.4 s on 30 worker processes. **The inherited draft could not have accepted a single real pair**: its split enum said `val` where 12.1.8 writes `validation`, its wireframe language was `tsx` where 12.1.6 and 12.1.7's `CHECKS` say `react`, and it refused the `split_basis`/`scribe` keys `splits.assign` adds to every record; its traversal cross-check split on raw `\|` and ignored 12.1.2's `\p` escaping, and its claimed 12-corruption mutation test did not exist - there are now **20 corruptions, each tripping `validate`**. Rules beyond key presence: `T\|` line equals `diagram_type`, language matches the type, validation/test pairs carry a scribe, synthetic stays in train, and **`target_code` may not contain `diagram_id`** - measured, **4,477 of 4,477 targets carried the diagram id** when emitted as 12.1.6 does (function/class/screen/netlist named after an id `ir_text` never shows, and for all 693 hdbpmn pages that id is the writer's), so every target is emitted against the canonical id `diagram` and **0 of 4,477** leak. flowchartseg is excluded, not forgotten: 1,319 pages with 0 edges. **Two genuine bugs fixed in committed modules**: `splits.assign` adopted the manifest's split for scribe-less rows, which would have put **381 + 361 writer-less didi pairs into validation/test** against its own train-only rule (now tested); and `targets.for_type` refused `er_diagram`, the IR schema's own enum value | ✅ |
| 12.1.2 | IR serialization format | Compact deterministic text (typed node/edge listing) over raw JSON, for token efficiency | `src/codegen/serialise.py` + `tests/test_codegen_serialise.py` (41 tests): `serialise(diagram, traversal) -> str`, with `parse` and `prune` so the format's losslessness is a testable property rather than a claim. Benchmarked with **Qwen2.5-Coder's own tokenizer** (local HF cache, `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, tokenizer files only, no weights, no GPU) over **400 real diagrams from all five sources, 11,156 nodes / 9,701 edges**. Median tokens per diagram: raw `.ir.json` **3,538**, pruned JSON 439, short-key JSON 416, keyed lines 275, **compact 253**. **The honest ratio is x1.61 against pruned JSON, not the x27.47 against raw**, and the docstring says so: the raw win is mostly deleting geometry, which any format would get, so quoting it would be crediting this encoding for `bbox` removal. **Two of the three baselines were unfair in the inherited draft and both were corrected against this module's own interest**: `prune` kept the edge `id` that compact rows never carry, charging JSON for information the encoding then got credit for (pruned x1.86 -> **x1.65**), while the short-key variant omitted edge direction and was winning by carrying less (x1.43 -> **x1.54**). **A determinism bug was found that the corpus structurally could not expose**: the edge sort key ended at `str(id or "")`, so two parallel `a->b` edges with no id were ordered by `list.sort` stability - i.e. by input position - and reversing `diagram["edges"]` reversed those rows. All 9,701 corpus edges happen to carry ids, so only 12.1.4's synthetic pairs would ever have hit it; the key is now `(rank_src, rank_dst, label, directed, id)` and the case is frozen in a test. Determinism is tested four ways - repeated calls, shuffled node/edge lists, dict key insertion order rebuilt, and `PYTHONHASHSEED` in {0, 1, 12345, random} compared by SHA-256 **in a real subprocess**, since in-process the seed is fixed per interpreter and the test cannot fail. **400/400 order-invariant, and `parse(serialise(d, t)) == prune(d, t)` on 400/400.** Preserved: diagram type, node id/shape/text/role, edge src/dst/label/directed, traversal order, edge multiplicity. Dropped with reasons: `bbox`/`polyline` (float noise and the classic repr-determinism leak), `confidence` (teaches the generator to hedge), edge `id` (multiplicity survives, identity does not), and `unresolved_edges`/`crossed_out`, because listing things that are not in the diagram invites the model to use them. Distinct from `src/assemble/serialise.py`, which is 10.2.4's JSON round-trip for the consumer's loader; both exist and the docstring says why | ✅ |
| 12.1.3 | Human-written targets | Reference code for all self-drawn diagrams | ≥260 pairs | ❌ |
| 12.1.4 | Synthetic pair generation | Random graphs → rendered as diagrams → reference code emitted programmatically | `src/codegen/synthetic.py` + `tests/test_codegen_synthetic.py` + `reports/codegen_synthetic.md`: **12,000 pairs, 2,400 per type** over flowchart / state_machine / er_diagram / wireframe / circuit, every one train-only (12.1.8), 12.1.1-valid and passed through 12.1.7's filter, each with a **rendered PNG** (12,000 in 64.5 s, 489 MB, 1.3.7's wobbled-stroke ink; samples of every type opened and inspected). **Consumer contract**: `python -m src.codegen.synthetic build` writes `data/processed/codegen/pairs/synthetic.jsonl` and re-merges, so `src.codegen.pairs.load_pairs("train")` now yields **train 16,139 (synthetic 12,000, didi 3,000, sketch2code 484, hdbpmn 451, fa_bresler 204) / validation 176 / test 162**; `load_pairs("train", sources=["synthetic"])` selects the synthetic set and `meta.image`/`meta.structure` give the PNG and family. Full build 164 s on 30 processes, and **byte-identical records rebuilt on 8 processes** (SHA-256 51a7abf7... both runs). **The inherited `src.synth.graphs` was measured first and cannot produce a training set**: over its own 12,500 diagrams circuits had **5 distinct structures** and ER **33** (targets only looked unique because the diagram id is in the code); ER columns and every circuit value and net lived in `attrs`, which 12.1.2's IR text drops, so those targets were invisible to the model; **0 of 500 of its diagrams are schema-valid IR** (float `ir_version`, type `er`, roles `source`/`resistor`); and 926 of 2,500 circuits do not solve. It is left untouched because 11.2 shares it; this module generates its own IR so the code is a function of `ir_text` - ER columns are `attribute` nodes, relationship edges carry 1/n or n/m, circuit nets are `wire` nodes with `+`/`-` terminal edges, wireframe containers are `div` only (other tags would live in invisible attrs) - validated against `schemas/ir.schema.json`. **Dedup is structural, not cosmetic**: exact `ir_text` duplicates dropped (806 across types, 641 of them wireframes), near-duplicates - same text-stripped structure signature and label Jaccard >= 0.8 - dropped (102), and at most 12 pairs per structure (2,751 capped). **Diversity of the kept set**: distinct structures flowchart 1,565 / state 2,142 / ER 1,810 / wireframe 2,162 / circuit 1,740, effective structures (exp entropy) 1,107 / 1,870 / 1,438 / 1,899 / 1,342, max 12 per structure, node p50 8 / 5 / 19 / 10 / 14 (max 22 / 9 / 44 / 87 / 27); family counts are in the report, with `linear` deliberately under-filled (93-474) because the cap stops its few shapes repeating. Flowcharts are 2,320 structured / 80 dispatch. **0 filter rejects** because circuits are built so every net has a DC path to ground and no inductor/source-only loop; ngspice still solved all 2,400. **Genuine bug fixed in `targets.emit_circuit_spice`**: nodes with role `wire` (the 2.1.5 vocabulary's net/junction) were emitted as phantom `R` cards. Two rendering bugs were found only by looking at the PNGs - `_text` silently skipped labels overflowing a narrow canvas, and BFS layering drew every circuit wire across the page - and fixed; dense wireframe rows can still overlap labels | ✅ |
| 12.1.5 | Sketch2Code pairs | Wireframe → HTML converted to React targets | `src/codegen/sketch2code.py` + `tests/test_codegen_sketch2code.py` (8 tests): **all 484 Sketch2Code webpages converted from HTML to a React component, and all 484 build with esbuild and render with `react-dom/server` in node** (12.3.6's harness, 17 s for the corpus). `html_to_jsx` walks `<body>` with `html.parser`: `class`/`for`/27 camel-cased DOM names renamed, `style` strings become style objects, text becomes `{"..."}` literals so page text can never become JSX syntax, void elements self-close, `<p>` is closed before a block as the HTML spec does, unclosed elements are closed; script/style/head/noscript/svg are dropped, as are **676 `on*` handler strings** (a string handler throws in React) and **76 non-JSX attribute names** - Alpine.js `:class` / `x-bind:` made **1 page fail to build** until they were dropped. **Checked by text fidelity, not by eye**: the rendered markup's visible text against the source body's, as word multisets **and** as whitespace-normalised textContent - **111,193 words, 0 lost, 0 invented, 484/484 textContent-equal**; 100 of 100 pages with one text node deleted were caught. **The first converter passed the word metric perfectly and was wrong**: it stripped text-node boundary whitespace, so `Hello <b>big</b> world` rendered `Hello<b>big</b>world` - word multisets matched on 484/484 while **textContent matched on 0/484** - which is why textContent is now the gate and the word metric is kept only beside it. **Output**: `data/processed/codegen/sketch2code_html/pairs.jsonl` (484 records, 12.1.1-valid, `source = sketch2code_html`, train-only, the 2.2.2 IR as `ir_text`), loaded with `src.codegen.sketch2code.load_pairs()`. **It is deliberately kept out of `pairs/` and the training merge, on measurement**: only **46.3%** of a target's words appear anywhere in its `ir_text` (the IR is 2.2.2's truncated structure summary), so these targets train exactly the invent-what-is-not-in-the-IR behaviour 12.2.4's contract forbids; and through Qwen2.5-Coder's chat template they are **p50 5,468 / p90 13,775 / max 36,115 tokens, 305 of 484 over 4,096 and 144 over 8,192**, with completions alone up to 30,018. They serve 12.3.6's render check and any long-context or retrieval experiment; the IR-faithful 12.1.6 React targets in `pairs/sketch2code.jsonl` remain the wireframe training source | ✅ |
| 12.1.6 | Target languages | Flowchart → Python; State machine → Python class / `transitions`; ER → SQL DDL; Wireframe → React + Tailwind; Circuit → SPICE netlist | `src/codegen/targets.py` + `tests/test_codegen_targets.py` (171 tests): `for_type(diagram_type)` and five `emit(diagram, traversal) -> str` generators, each verified by the checker for its language rather than by eye. **Measured over 5,796 real diagrams and 2,500 synthetic ones, every emitter is at 1.0000**: hdbpmn 693/693, flowchartseg 1,319/1,319, didi 3,000/3,000 and fa_bresler 300/300 through `ast.parse` **and** `compile()`, sketch2code 484/484 React, and 500/500 for each of the five on synthetic input, at 0.04-2.17 ms per diagram. **ER is checked by `src.eval.sql.check`, not by execution**, because SQLite accepts a foreign key pointing at a table that does not exist - 1,696 FKs and 296 junction tables resolve over 500 diagrams with zero placeholder fallbacks, so it is not empty-schema padding. **Coverage is stated rather than implied, and the brief was wrong in this project's favour**: three languages have real diagrams behind them - flowchart (5,012 pages), **state machine (fa_bresler's 300 finite automata, which `sequences.LABELLED_SOURCES` already treats as labelled)** and wireframe (484) - while **ER and circuit have no real diagrams anywhere in the repo and are synthetic-only, so their numbers are well-formedness results and not fidelity results**. Three real bugs were found by the corpus, not by inspection: an empty Python suite when every node on a page mapped to a comment-only statement broke `ast.parse` on **9 of 693 hdbpmn pages**; `_ident` escaped Python keywords but not SQL ones, so entity names like `order`, `group` and `table` were bare syntax errors in **77 of 200 synthetic ER diagrams**; and SPICE nets appearing on a single card left dangling wires in **40 of 200 circuits**. **`transitions` is not installed here, so no import of it is emitted** - a test asserts the string never appears - and the class instead carries `STATES`/`TRANSITIONS`/`TABLE` in exactly `transitions.Machine`'s shape, one constructor call from the library form. Robustness is 12 hostile diagrams x 5 emitters: empty, edgeless, self-loop, cycle, disconnected, unresolved edges with `None` and dangling ids, garbled OCR (`\|\|\|~~`, `class`, `3 items`, CJK), duplicate labels, missing id, 60-deep and cyclic containment. **Three claims in the inherited docstring were deleted rather than adjusted**: a 92.4% structured-emission rate that actually measures 71.4%, a 0.0% synthetic fallback that is 1.8%, and a `@babel/parser` agreement of 1.000 on 1,000 targets that cannot have been produced here - there is no `package.json` and no `node_modules`, and the JSX check is an offline structural parse | ✅ |
| 12.1.7 | Quality filter | Every target must parse or compile; reject otherwise | `src/codegen/quality.py` + `src/eval/spice.py` + `src/eval/react.py` (toolchain + batch runner) + `tests/test_codegen_quality.py` + `tests/test_eval_spice.py` (21 tests): `filter_pairs` partitions and `assert_all_compile` re-checks the survivors from scratch and raises on one miss, and **`src.codegen.pairs.write_shard` calls both before any shard is written**, rejections going to `data/processed/codegen/rejected/<source>.jsonl` - so 100% is a postcondition of the only path into `pairs/`. **Measured on the 4,477 real pairs: 4,477 kept, 0 rejected** (3,655 Python, 484 React rendered, 21 s for build + filter + re-check). Each language gets the strongest checker available here rather than a proxy: Python `ast.parse` **and** `compile()` (`return 1` and `def f(a, a)` both pass `ast.parse`); SQL through 12.3.7's FK-resolving `src.eval.sql.check`; **React through a real esbuild 0.25.10 build plus `react-dom/server` `renderToString` in a node `vm` sandbox with a timeout** (484 components in 1.3 s, one node process per 500); **SPICE through ngspice 47 batch mode**, which must parse the deck *and* solve its `.op` point. **The simulator is the finding**: run over the 12,500 pairs the inherited `src.synth.graphs` generator produces, **926 of 2,500 circuits (37.0%) pass every structural rule and are physically unsolvable** - singular DC matrices from series capacitors with no DC path and parallel ideal inductors - while Python 5,000, SQL 2,500 and React 2,500 all pass (12,500 checked in 26.4 s). A structural-only filter would have trained on all 926. The self-test is 37 cases (15 valid, 22 invalid across five languages) with **0 false accepts and 0 false rejects**, and 6 of the invalid cases are clean to the static checker and caught only by the tool (undefined identifier at render, a missing import, `1 +;`; parallel inductors, a DC-floating node, an undefined diode model). A missing node or ngspice **rejects** (`react.unavailable`/`spice.unavailable`), so an environment without the tools cannot produce a shard claiming to compile. **Inherited claims found false or stale**: it executed SQL with `executescript`, which accepts `REFERENCES nosuch(id)` and a `BANANA` column type (both now rejections), one of its own "valid" SQL cases had no primary key and is rejected by 12.3.7, its `_schema_complaint` swallowed every exception from the schema import, and its self-test records could not pass 12.1.1's schema. Tools live in gitignored `data/processed/codegen/{node,tools}`, installed by `react.ensure_toolchain()` / `spice.ensure_toolchain()` with pinned versions | ✅ |
| 12.1.8 | Train/val/test split | Held-out diagrams *and* held-out scribes | `src/codegen/splits.py` + `tests/test_codegen_splits.py` (25 tests): `assign(records)` fills `split`, `split_basis` and `scribe` over 2,077 pairs - **train 1,739 / validation 176 / test 162**, and on the 993 scribed pairs alone 655 / 176 / 162. **Zero scribe overlap and zero diagram overlap are recomputed independently in the tests** by intersecting the three scribe sets and the three diagram-id sets pairwise rather than reading `summarize`'s own verdict: all intersections empty over 130 scribes and 2,077 distinct ids. **The split is adopted, not re-derived**, which is the whole point - hdbpmn comes from 1.3.3's `manifest.parquet` verbatim and agrees with the label-crop index on **693/693** pages, and fa_bresler now adopts `src.detect.dataset.fa_splits`, the split `src/detect/**` already trains on, after checking both ways and finding **300/300 diagrams and 25/25 writers agree** - so agreement is now a mechanism rather than a coincidence. **A defect in 1.3's own splitter was found and is recorded rather than silently worked around**: `src/ingest/splits.py` buckets writer-less rows on `int.from_bytes(id.encode()[:8], 'little') % 100`, and every sketch2code id begins `sketch2code/`, so all **731 rows share those 8 bytes, hash to bucket 71, and the manifest holds 727 validation, 4 test and 0 train**. Adopting that would have put essentially all of sketch2code in val, so this module does not bridge to it; the real fix is hashing the full id, which is a Phase 1.3 change and was left unmade rather than smuggled in here. **Records with no writer identity go to train only** - synthetic and sketch2code alike - because a pair with no scribe cannot be held out from a scribe and in val/test would only dilute the claim those splits exist to support, and because 10K synthetic pairs against ~150 real held-out pages would make val loss a number about a generator; `heldout_pairs_all_have_a_scribe` asserts it. **The leak tests poison the index rather than the output**, so they exercise the real path: an index placing one writer's two diagrams on opposite sides is caught as `leaking_scribes`, and the suite is mutation-verified - hashing scribe-less records across three splits fails 5 tests, splitting per-diagram instead of per-writer fails 2. **The inherited draft's docstring numbers were stale**: it claimed 1497/266/314 over 163 scribes where its own unmodified code produced 1590/245/242 over 130, and it hashed 149 writer-less pairs into val and test | ✅ |

### 12.2 Fine-Tuning

| # | Task | Detail | Definition of Done | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| 12.2.1 | Base model choice | 7B code model (Qwen2.5-Coder-7B / CodeLlama-7B / DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B) — benchmark zero-shot first | `src/llm/benchmark.py` + `src/llm/generate.py` + `src/llm/score.py` + `src/llm/functional.py` + `reports/llm_benchmark.md`: every candidate loaded as it would be fine-tuned (NF4, bf16 compute, SDPA - flags read back: use_cache on in config and generation_config, left padding, pad token set), fed identical messages (the template loaded from its git object, so a working-tree edit cannot differ between candidates; Qwen's 176 prompt token counts re-derived 176/176), greedy, over all 176 real validation pairs with one denominator. Each run's batch was chosen from a measured ms/sample sweep on a fixed length-stratified sample. **Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct**: contract 91.5%, syntax 96.6%, executes 58.5%, functional pass@1 **13.6%** (fa_bresler 22.9%, hdbpmn 10.2%), dropped-node rate 31.1%, 2 replies hit the 1,600-token limit; ms/sample by batch x8 7.4 s, x16 17.9 s, x32 3.9 s -> batch 32, split in 1364.8 s; **deepseek-ai/deepseek-coder-6.7b-instruct**: contract 12.5%, syntax 60.8%, executes 26.7%, functional pass@1 **1.1%** (fa_bresler 4.2%, hdbpmn 0.0%), dropped-node rate 89.1%, 73 replies hit the 1,600-token limit; ms/sample by batch x8 26.0 s -> batch 8, split in 5982.8 s. **Selected Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct** on functional pass@1, validation only. Functional pass@1 is 12.3.3's per-diagram test against the drawing (IR automaton for state machines, reachability / order / exclusivity for flowcharts), under which the 12.1.6 reference programs themselves pass only 25.8% of hdbpmn and 27.1% of fa_bresler validation pairs - the ceiling a model imitating them is under. CodeLlama-7B-Instruct was downloaded but not benchmarked: two families were compared and its GPU hour went to the fine-tune | ✅ |
| 12.2.2 | Quantization | 4-bit NF4 QLoRA to fit RTX 4500 Ada (24 GB) | `src/llm/quant.py` + `src/llm/train.py` + `tests/test_llm_quant.py` + `tests/test_llm_train.py` (11 tests, CPU only) + `reports/llm_quant.md`: `load_model` (NF4, double quant, bf16 compute, SDPA) is the one loader every 12.2 row uses, `profile` runs real forward/backward/AdamW steps over sequence length x micro-batch x arm under a **20 GiB process cap** so the WDDM cliff raises OOM instead of spilling to host memory, and `runtime_checks` reads the flags back from the objects. Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct: **196 `Linear4bit` nf4 modules, 5.60 GB on device, 40.37 M LoRA parameters (r=16, all seven projections)**. **Measured flags**: SDPA resolves, but torch 2.5.1's Windows build has the flash kernel compiled out (mem-efficient and cuDNN run) and `flash-attn`/`xformers` are absent. **Gradient checkpointing was silently off**: `from_pretrained` returns an eval-mode model and HF checkpoints only when `training`, so the flag read True while 512 tokens x2 took **19.08 GB vs 8.54 GB** in train mode; the profile now records `gradient_checkpointing_effective`. **Saturation is micro-batch 1 at every length** (1024: x1 968, x2 989, x4 938 tokens/s, x8 OOM; 2048: 967 / 918 / OOM; 4096: 888 / OOM) - the step is compute-bound on 4-bit dequantisation, so effective batch comes from accumulation, not VRAM. **Rejected on measurement**: `peft.prepare_model_for_kbit_training` upcasts the 1.09 B-parameter embedding/`lm_head` and norms to fp32 and runs **2.9x slower** (336 vs 968 tokens/s); no checkpointing is 1.41x faster but fits only 1024 x1 at 18.71 GB while real pairs reach 3,696 tokens; unquantised bf16 LoRA is +7% (981 vs 916 tokens/s) at 20.61 GB reserved, over the cap. **The trained configuration fits with headroom**: `train.selected_loss` applies `lm_head` only at completion positions and packs rows first-fit-decreasing into 2,048-token bins with a block-diagonal causal mask and restarted position ids - on real packed train rows **916 tokens/s at 8.41 GB allocated / 10.83 GB reserved of 24 GiB (~13 GB free)**, against 10.30 GB with full logits. **Packing isolation was verified, not assumed**: three examples packed score 0.7206 vs 0.7191 apart, and the same row under a plain causal mask scores 2.4969; a tiny-Qwen2 CPU test pins packed == apart and mutation-tests the mask. Examples longer than the bin get their own uncut row (97 of 655), 0 truncated | ✅ |
| 12.2.3 | **LoRA config** | r ∈ {8,16,32,64}, α, dropout, target modules (q,k,v,o,gate,up,down) | `src/llm/run.py` + `src/llm/train.py` + `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `configs/llm.yaml` + `reports/llm_lora_sweep.md` + `reports/figures/p12_lora_sweep.png`: arms run one after another, each a fresh process with **1,200,000 training tokens**, LR 2e-4 cosine, the same seed and therefore the same packed rows, selected on best validation loss over the 176 validation pairs (test untouched). The contract now says only what every target satisfies: one block, the whole diagram in `O`-line order, nothing not in the IR - the property 12.1.1 (no diagram id in code) and 12.1.4 (columns and nets moved out of attrs) were rebuilt to make true. Threshold >= 95%: met | ✅ |
