Threshold >= 85%: met | ✅ |
| 12.3.3 | **Functional correctness** | pass@1 against per-diagram unit tests | `src/llm/score.py` + `src/llm/functional.py` + `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `reports/llm_quality.md`: all 162 LoRA test generations, one denominator (unparseable replies count as failures everywhere). `src/llm/functional.py` + `tests/test_llm_functional.py`: per-diagram tests derived from the drawing, run in 11.2.9's sandbox - state machines: every word of length 0-3 over the edge-label alphabet against the IR automaton (comma labels are two symbols, epsilon closure); flowcharts: the complete decision tree to depth 12 with recording stubs, passing iff every reachable drawn operation runs on some path, no path reverses the drawn order, and no path runs two exclusive branches. Mutation-verified on the validation references: dropped operations pass 3.1%, swapped operations 17.4%, redirected transitions 22.9%, flipped accepting sets 10.6%, while branch-polarity swaps pass at the reference's own rate. **pass@1 70.4%** (fa_bresler 66.7%, hdbpmn 71.9%); the reference programs pass 78.4% under the same tests, because 12.1.6's emitter collapses `a,b` into one trigger and serialises forks as if/else. Failure reasons {'verdicts_differ': 15, 'equal': 114, 'syntax': 1, 'exclusive_branches_both_ran': 3, 'missing_operation': 16, 'order_violation': 7, 'never_returns': 1, 'crash_or_nontermination': 4, 'candidate_sandbox': 1}. **The 70% gate previously applied here sat above the reference ceiling and scored 12.1.6's emitter rather than the model**; the criterion is the references' own rate under the same tests (78.4%): not met | ❌ |
| 12.3.4 | Structural fidelity | Do generated branches / states / tables match the IR? (AST-vs-graph comparison) | `src/eval/structural.py` + `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `reports/llm_structural.md`: the AST-vs-graph comparison run over 162 LoRA test generations. **Structural fidelity 0.8263** against the reference ceiling 0.8267 (flowchart 0.8714, state_machine 0.7194). Everything expected is derived from the `ir_text` the model was shown, never from the IR file's `attrs`, so the metric cannot reward a model for reproducing what it could not see; correspondence is exact-match on 12.1.6's own identifier function, because a fuzzy rule lets one code name match nine drawn boxes | ✅ |
| 12.3.5 | Similarity metrics | CodeBLEU / exact match / edit distance to reference | `src/eval/similarity.py` + `tests/test_eval_similarity.py` + `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `reports/llm_similarity.md`: CodeBLEU (all four components, self-contained - no `codebleu` package), exact match and token edit distance over 162 LoRA test generations. **CodeBLEU 0.933223, exact match 0.598765, edit similarity 0.919641**; the references against themselves score 1.0, which is the metric's ceiling on this corpus. A component undefined for a language (no JSX/SQL/SPICE parser in the stdlib) is dropped and the remaining weights renormalised rather than scored as zero, so a perfect SQL answer is 1.000 and not 0.500; every row carries which components it had. **The calibration is the finding: renaming every local variable - which changes no behaviour - costs CodeBLEU 0.5757, while deleting a statement costs only 0.9754**, so the metric is ordered against correctness and is reported as a comparative number only; 12.3.3 is where correctness is settled | ✅ |
| 12.3.6 | React render check | Headless build and render of generated components | `src/eval/react.py` + `tests/test_eval_react.py` (6 tests): `check_many(codes, render=True)` runs a **real headless build and render** in one node 22 process per batch - esbuild 0.25.10 JSX transform, then the CommonJS module evaluated in a fresh `vm` context whose only importable module is `react`, and `react-dom/server` `renderToString` on the default export, module body and render both under a `vm` timeout - returning `kind` in `react.ok / build_error / no_component / render_error / timeout / crash / unavailable`. Pinned versions are installed by `ensure_toolchain()` into gitignored `data/processed/codegen/node`; nothing under `node_modules` is committed, and a missing toolchain is `react.unavailable`, never a pass. **Every React target in the repo renders without error: 3,368 of 3,368** - 12.1.6's real sketch2code targets 484/484 (3.07 ms each), synthetic 2,400/2,400 (2.25 ms) and 12.1.5's converted webpages 484/484 (4.08 ms), so a model's 2,000-component eval costs seconds. **Deliberately broken components are rejected, 2,400 of 2,400**: 8 defects injected into 300 real components each (150 synthetic, 100 sketch2code, 50 converted pages), each landing in the kind it should - dropped closing tag and `1 +;` as `build_error`, an undefined identifier, an import of a missing module, a throw in render and returning an object as `render_error`, `while (true)` as `timeout`, no export as `no_component`. **The number that justifies node over a parser: 12.1.7's structural JSX check rejects only 600 of those 2,400** (the dropped tag and the missing export) and passes **all 1,800** of the undefined-identifier, missing-import, invalid-expression, infinite-loop, throw and returns-object components. The tests pin each mutation to its exact kind and assert that four of them are structurally clean yet fail to render. **Limits, stated**: server rendering runs no effects or event handlers and has no `window`/`document`, so a component that only fails in a browser passes and one that touches the DOM at render time is rejected; Tailwind class names are not validated; and a timeout costs its full budget (the study's 300 infinite loops are most of its 5.5 minutes). The LLM agent calls `check_many` on extracted model outputs for 12.3.6 model numbers | ✅ |
| 12.3.7 | SQL check | Execute DDL on SQLite/Postgres, verify FKs and cardinality | `src/eval/sql.py` + `tests/test_eval_sql.py` (22 tests): `check(ddl) -> {ok, kind, ...}` with `kind` in `sql.ok/execution_error/empty_schema/foreign_key_error/cardinality_error/type_error/unavailable`. **Execution alone is nearly worthless as a gate here, and that is measured rather than asserted** - two tests pin the premise directly: SQLite accepts `REFERENCES nosuch(id)` against a table that does not exist, reports `PRAGMA foreign_keys` as 1 and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` as empty; and it accepts `CREATE TABLE t(x TEXTT, y BANANA)`, storing both invented type names verbatim with BLOB affinity. A checker that reports the absence of an exception passes both. **Everything of value is the static pass that runs after execution succeeds**: every FK's parent table and column must exist and the parent column set must be UNIQUE or the primary key (SQLite raises `foreign key mismatch` only at DML time, Postgres at DDL time); cardinality is classified from the child side as one-to-one / one-to-many, with a table whose primary key is the union of two or more FK columns reported as a junction table; and declared type names are matched against the SQL92-plus-SQLite set. **An empty schema is a failure, not a pass** - it is the likeliest way for a broken generator to score 100%. **One real bug was found and fixed while testing**: `split_statements` accumulated whole lines, so two statements sharing one line became a single buffer that `complete_statement` called complete and `execute` then refused - a schema rejected for the checker's own bug and reported as `sql.execution_error`, which is the worst failure mode a checker has. It masked the non-unique-parent case entirely; scanning to each terminator fixes it and keeps the `BEGIN ... END` trigger body intact, both pinned by tests. Cost is **0.41 ms mean per call** (median 0.39, max 1.00 over 1,000 calls), with a regression guard in the suite. **Rejected**: `executescript`, which wraps the batch in one implicit transaction where `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is documented as a no-op, so the pragma the module depends on would have been silently ignored; a `sqlglot`/`sqlparse` dependency, because the row's point is that the engine is the authority on what the DDL actually built; and treating a nullable FK as an error, since optional participation is a legitimate model and is reported in `relationships[*].optional` instead. **Postgres is refused, not faked**: no `psycopg`, `psycopg2` or `psql` in this environment, so `dialect="postgres"` returns `kind=sql.unavailable` rather than silently falling back to SQLite, because a Postgres pass that was really a SQLite pass is worse than no number at all. Every number in this row is SQLite | ✅ |
| 12.3.8 | Hallucination audit | Nodes invented or dropped by the model | `src/llm/score.py` + `src/llm/functional.py` + `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `reports/llm_quality.md`: all 162 LoRA test generations, one denominator (unparseable replies count as failures everywhere). Dropped = drawn operations (flowchart `process` nodes) or states that no called/defined name or literal in the program names, via the functional driver's resolver; invented = called or defined operation names that resolve to no drawn node, excluding condition predicates and scaffolding. **Dropped-node rate 16.8% (24.1% of programs drop at least one), 0.864 invented names per program (32.1% of programs)**; reference programs: dropped 16.8%, invented 0.852 per program | ✅ |
| 12.3.9 | Repair loop | On failure, feed the error back for one repair attempt | `src/llm/pipeline.py` + `reports/llm_repair.md`: every failing LoRA test program gets exactly one repair turn - its own reply plus the failure in words (parser message, sandbox exception kind, or the functional test's reason) - same adapter, greedy, batch from a measured sweep (8, 26041.7 ms/sample). **1 of 48 failures fixed (2.1% success-after-repair); pass@1 70.4% -> 71.0% after one repair** over all 162 test pairs; 2 repaired replies no longer parse. Remaining reasons {'verdicts_differ': 15, 'syntax': 2, 'equal': 1, 'missing_operation': 15, 'order_violation': 7, 'never_returns': 1, 'crash_or_nontermination': 4, 'exclusive_branches_both_ran': 2, 'candidate_sandbox': 1} | ✅ |

# Phase 13 — End-to-End Pipeline Orchestration

| # | Task | Detail | Definition of Done | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| 13.1 | Pipeline object | `DreamScriptPipeline.run(image) -> {ir, code, artifacts}` | `src/pipeline/core.py`: `DreamScriptPipeline.run(image)` composes Phases 9-12 into eight stages - load, detect, classify, assemble, traverse, serialise, generate, verify - and returns one `Result` carrying the IR, the code, the language and a per-stage table. **Over the 25 held-out golden pages (`tests/fixtures/p13_golden.json`, stratified 8 fa_bresler / 17 hdbpmn in the proportion of the test split), 25/25 produce code that passes 12.3.1's parse gate**, none stops early and none needs confirmation (`runs/p13_golden/summary.json`). The stage boundary is 13.2's `Outcome`, so a stage that cannot answer returns a reason rather than raising and `run` reports `stopped_at` with the timings that did accrue - a failure is still a measurement **Amended after 13.10 measured behaviour rather than parsing: "produces code" here means the parse gate, and for state machines that is a weaker claim than it sounds.** On a golden fa_bresler page the annotation carries 4 nodes, 6 edges and the triggers `a`, `b`, `a,b`; assembly recovers the 4 nodes but emits **21-25 edges with every `label` empty**, because nothing in the assembler reads edge labels. With no triggers every transition is an epsilon transition, so epsilon-closure correctly merges all four states into one and the emitted machine has **1 state and 0 transitions** - it parses, and it cannot accept or reject anything. The emitter is not at fault; the IR is empty of edge labels. **Half of that is now fixed**: the pipeline was not running any of the three stages S5 is actually scored with - `statelabels`, `_prune_short_loops`, `_orient` - and wiring them in `src/pipeline/vision.py` took node text from **0 to 386 of 295 annotated** and cut edge over-segmentation from **648 to 518 against 397 annotated**. The machines are still vacuous, because **edge labels remain 0 of 113**: that is the open item, and it is recoverable, since the truth exists in the corpus and `labelcrops.elements_of` already treats an edge as a first-class element with a box at its polyline midpoint | ✅ |
| 13.2 | Stage contracts | Typed dataclasses between every stage | `src/pipeline/contracts.py`: `Outcome` / `StageReport` / `Result` as **pydantic dataclasses**, not `BaseModel`s - `StageReport("detect", ok=True, ...)` is constructed positionally throughout the pipeline, which a `BaseModel` forbids, and `pydantic.dataclasses.dataclass` validates without changing a call site. **Validation is shallow on payloads and strict on structure, deliberately**: `Outcome.value` is `Any` under `arbitrary_types_allowed` because what crosses that boundary is a numpy array or an IR dict already validated by `schemas/ir.schema.json` where it is produced, and restating that schema here would check nothing new; but `seconds`/`confidence` are floats and `stages` is a list of `StageReport`, because those are what the timing table and budget check are built from - a stage writing a string into `seconds` would sort a table wrongly and compare a string to 10.0. Demonstrated: a string in `seconds` and a non-`StageReport` in `stages` both raise `ValidationError`, while an int coerces to float. **`mypy src/pipeline` is clean - 'no issues found in 9 source files' - and getting there found four real defects**, all the `LoosePage`/`Page` duck-typing this package relies on and had only asserted in prose: `corpus.Page` derives `image` from its split so a loose page cannot subclass it, which is now a `PageLike` Protocol naming the three members the tracer actually reads, with explicit casts at the two boundaries instead of a silent type error | ✅ |
| 13.3 | Routing logic | NB fast prior → ensemble classifier → type-specific parser and codegen | `src/pipeline/routing.py`: multinomial naive Bayes over the detector's own class histogram - literally the plan's "NB fast prior", fitted on evidence the pipeline has already paid for by the time it routes. `fit` trains on val, `evaluate` scores test, and both are disjoint from the detector's training split, so the number is not the detector remembering its own pages. **Tested per type on held-out test: flowchart 114/114, state_machine 48/48, overall 162/162 = 1.0000.** That is perfect because the question is genuinely easy, and the row says so rather than claiming a hard win: the two vocabularies barely overlap - a flowchart page averages **69.97 arrowheads, 6.45 rounded-rects and 3.39 diamonds with no double-circle**, a state-machine page **3.35 circles and 1.56 double-circles and essentially nothing else**. **The coverage gap is the real limitation and is not hidden: only 2 of the 5 types have annotated detector data anywhere in this repo** - 9.1.2's export is hdbpmn, flowchartseg and fa_bresler, while er_diagram and circuit have no real annotated pages (12.1.6 records the same gap) and sketch2code's wireframes are not in the detection corpus. Those three are routed to `unknown` with confidence 0.0 and handed to 13.5's gate, **not forced into whichever of the two trained types they resemble least**, and that refusal path is what the tests pin. One honest caveat on the posterior: it saturates - the minimum over all 162 test pages is **0.9999** - because NB multiplies ~70 counts it assumes independent, so 13.5's 0.60 floor is inert for these two types and earns its keep only on the `unknown` path. **Amended: that 162/162 was measured on cached corpus detections, and the pipeline does not route those.** `corpus.detections` includes arrowheads; `pipeline.vision.detect_boxes` filtered them, because the tracer does not want them - and the arrowhead count is this prior's strongest feature by a wide margin, so the fitted histogram and the served one were different objects. A train/serve skew, and not a theoretical one: golden page `hdbpmn__ex08_writer0096` carries 92 boxes including **67 arrowheads** and routes `flowchart` at 1.0, while the same page with arrowheads stripped carries 24 boxes and routes **`state_machine` at 0.9909** - past 13.5's gate, emitting a Python state machine for a BPMN diagram, and it was the one golden state machine still producing zero transitions, so the routing bug and the vacuous-machine bug were the same bug. `detect_boxes` now takes `keep_arrowheads`, the pipeline classifies on the full list and `assemble_diagram` drops them before tracing, so assembly sees exactly what it saw before. **End-to-end on live detections the golden set goes 24/25 -> 25/25**, and that is the number that describes what the pipeline does; the 162/162 above describes the prior in isolation and both are now stated rather than one standing in for the other | ✅ |
| 13.4 | Fallback chain | Handcrafted classifier fails → embedding classifier → CNN; OCR fails → placeholder identifiers | **Stays ❌, and the evidence for why is in `reports/p13_fallback_chain.json` rather than a green phrasing.** Two of the three chains this row names are built and demonstrated. *OCR fails → placeholder identifiers* degrades in three rungs, each verified to still produce code that passes 12.3.1: the read label (`q0/q1/q2`) → the node id (`s14/s15/s16`) → `_ident`'s literal fallback. *Model fails → emitter* is `src/pipeline/generate.py`, which marks an emitter answer `degraded=True` so no measurement mixes the two. **The classifier chain cannot be built on the data that survived, and building it would make the pipeline worse.** The handcrafted rung itself is real and good - rebuilt `handcrafted.parquet` (1,435 rows, 33 features, 2.5 pages/s) scores **0.9961 ± 0.0028 accuracy and 0.9960 macro F1 out-of-fold**, grouped by writer over 5 folds × 3 seeds - and it would fix a genuine defect: the NB router calls sketch2code wireframes **flowchart at 0.9965 and 0.9999**, sailing past 13.5's 0.60 gate, where this rung says wireframe at 0.9998/1.0000/0.9995 out-of-fold. But it has **no state_machine class**, and on the 8 golden fa_bresler pages it predicts **wireframe 6/8 at up to 0.9978 confidence**, so no threshold separates it: wiring it trades the router's correct 48/48 state machines for a wireframe label no downstream stage can consume. The class cannot be added honestly - **fa_bresler's raw data is 300 inkml stroke files, not photographs**, so its pages are rendered ink while every row in the table is a photograph or scan, and a 3-class model would be separating rendering domain rather than diagram type. That confound is already visible: the 2-class model groups rendered fa_bresler with clean sketch2code line drawings. The embedding and CNN rungs sit on the same two-class data, and the synthetic corpus that covered all five types evenly is **0 rows** after the rebuild | ❌ |
| 13.5 | Confidence gating | Below threshold ⇒ ask the user to confirm rather than guess | `src/pipeline/core.py`: `CONFIDENCE_FLOOR = 0.60` on 13.3's routing posterior, below which the result comes back `needs_confirmation=True` with **no code at all**, because every stage after routing is type-specific - emitting Python for what is actually an ER diagram produces a confident, runnable, wrong answer, which is worse than asking. The floor is a constructor argument rather than a constant because the right value depends on whether a human is present to answer, and the CLI exposes it as `--confidence-floor`. Two tests pin the behaviour: a 0.51 posterior under a 0.9 floor stops at `classify` with empty code, and an `unknown` type is never forced into whichever of the two trained classes it resembles least. On the golden set the gate fires on **0 of 25** pages, so it costs nothing where routing is confident | ✅ |
| 13.6 | Caching | Per-stage cache keyed on image hash + config hash | `src/pipeline/cache.py`: one JSON per (stage, key), keyed on `sha256(image bytes)` plus a hash of the configuration **plus a digest of the code that produced the value**. Measured over the 25 golden pages with the cache deleted first (`reports/p13_cache.json`): **cold median 5.545 s / p90 6.692 s / max 9.343 s and 144.53 s of stage work in total, against a warm run of 0.00 s with 150 of 150 lookups hitting.** The speed-up is deliberately not quoted as a ratio - a fully warm run does no stage work at all, so every per-stage time falls under the timer's resolution and the quotient is an artefact of dividing by ~0; the first version of this report printed 5.5e9, which measures the clock rather than the cache. Hashing the *bytes* matters on this corpus: 1.3.2 found nine duplicate groups across 25 images. **The code digest is the part that was missing and it was a correctness bug, not a refinement.** The key was the image plus three constructor arguments and nothing about the pipeline, so wiring the S5 assembly stages in changed what `assemble` returns for every page while a warm cache went on serving the old IR - a state machine rebuilt with 5 states and 16 real transitions still came back with one merged state and zero, which is exactly what this module's docstring promised could not happen. It was found by a spot check disagreeing with a measurement, not by reading the code. `code_key` digests the source of `src/pipeline`, `assemble`, `codegen`, `ocr` and `parse` - source text rather than mtimes, since a checkout or a rebase moves mtimes without changing behaviour - at **7.1 ms once per process** against stage work measured in seconds, and two tests pin it | ✅ |
| 13.7 | Latency budget | Target < 10 s end-to-end on GPU; per-stage timing table | `runs/p13_golden/summary.json`: **median 0.411 s, p90 0.692 s, max 3.296 s over 25 pages against the plan's < 10 s budget - 25/25 inside it**. Reported per stage rather than as one number, because the two expensive stages are the two a deployment would move: median uncached **assemble 0.388 s**, **detect 0.044 s**, verify 0.001 s, and classify / traverse / serialise / generate each below a millisecond. **Assembly, not the detector, is the cost** - 10.1.3's skeleton walk dominates, which is the opposite of what a vision pipeline is usually assumed to spend its time on. The 3.296 s maximum is the first page and is model load, not per-page work. **The budget is met with the emitter answering, not a served model**: 13.4 records that no llama.cpp server was reachable for this run, and a served 7B would add 12.2's measured ~10.7 s/sample, which would breach the budget on its own - so the number is honest about what produced it **Superseded, and by a change that was worth the cost.** Those numbers were measured before assembly ran `statelabels` and `edgelabels`, which is to say before the pipeline produced a state machine that does anything. Re-measured on a quiet machine over the same 25 pages (`reports/p13_cache.json`): **cold median 5.545 s, p90 6.692 s, max 9.343 s - 25/25 still inside the 10 s budget, but on 0.66 s of headroom at the maximum rather than the 6.7 s the old figure implied.** The whole increase is one stage: **assemble 0.388 s -> 5.4577 s median**, two TrOCR decodes per page, while detect stays at 0.0717 s and every other stage is under a millisecond. That is what took the generated machines from 9 of 9 vacuous to 0 of 8, so it is a price paid for correctness rather than a regression - but the budget is now genuinely tight, and **it is met only with 12.1.6's emitter answering**: 12.2.9 measures a served Q4_K_M at p50 6.678 s, which would put end-to-end p50 near 12.2 s and breach the budget outright. 13.6's warm cache is what makes a re-run free | ✅ |
| 13.8 | Error handling | Every stage fails soft with an actionable message | `tests/test_pipeline_fuzz.py`: 8 property tests, 60 generated examples each, over three layers - arbitrary file bytes written as a `.png`, malformed detector output (NaN and inf geometry, wrong types, missing keys, absurd magnitudes), and a detector that raises `RuntimeError` / `ValueError` / `KeyError` / `MemoryError` / `OSError`. **The invariant is that `run` returns a `Result` at every layer** - possibly one that stopped at the first stage with a reason, which is the right answer to a corrupt page, but never a traceback reaching the caller. The detector is stubbed rather than fuzzed, because loading YOLO per example would measure ultralytics instead of this pipeline's error handling. **The first version of this test was weaker than it looked and is worth recording**: it wrote a header-only PNG, so `cv2.imread` returned None and all sixty examples stopped at `assemble` - it passed while exercising one branch repeatedly. With a genuinely decodable page the examples spread across `detect`, `assemble` and running all the way to verified code, which is what makes the invariant worth asserting | ✅ |
| 13.9 | Batch mode CLI | `dreamscript run <dir> --out <dir>` | `src/pipeline/cli.py`: `dreamscript run <dir> --out <dir>` (also `python -m src.pipeline run`, which is what the tests drive so the suite does not need an editable install). One page or a directory; writes per page the generated source in its own language (`.py` / `.sql` / `.jsx` / `.cir`) plus a JSON record of every stage, and over the batch a `summary.json` and a `report.md`. **25/25 golden pages produced code, median 0.411 s.** Serial by design - one resident detector and one resident model on one card, and 13.6's cache is what makes a re-run cheap, so parallel pages would contend for the card and race on the cache while the measurement the CLI supports (13.7's budget) is per page anyway. A page that stops early is still written out with its `stopped_at` and partial stage table, and the process exits non-zero **only when no page produced code**, which is the case where the run is wrong rather than the page | ✅ |
| 13.10 | Integration tests | 25 golden images → expected IR + expected code behaviour | `tests/test_pipeline_golden.py`: 7 tests, green, and they assert **behaviour rather than parsing** - which is the distinction that made this row worth building. The old suite checked that every golden page produced code and that the code parsed, and **that check passes on a program that does nothing**: on a golden state machine whose annotation carries the triggers `a`, `b`, `a,b`, assembly emitted edges with every `label` empty, every transition became an epsilon transition, epsilon-closure merged all four states into one, and the machine had one state and no transitions. It parsed. It could not accept or reject a single string, and **9 of 9 generated state machines were like that**. The IR side is checked against the annotation's own counts (`reports/p13_golden_behaviour.json`: 397 predicted nodes against 360 annotated, 518 edges against 397) and the behaviour side executes the emitted module and drives it through its own transition table, so a machine that transitions nowhere fails instead of passing. **It is now 0 of 8 vacuous**, and the two bugs that produced the nine are both fixed and both recorded: nothing read edge labels (`src/assemble/edgelabels.py`, 0 -> 442 of 113 annotated) and a BPMN page routed to `state_machine` at 0.9909 because the router was served a histogram with arrowheads stripped out (13.3). A ceiling test keeps the honest failure visible rather than hidden: **442 labels against 113 annotated is 3.91x over-production**, assembly's edge over-segmentation showing through, so the machines run and carry spurious transitions beside the real ones - the bound is set just above the measurement to catch a regression, explicitly not because 3.91x is acceptable | ✅ |

Units 1-3 are fully covered, and Unit 4 now carries four of its five requirements: clustering (Phase 8), CNN (Phase 9), **reinforcement learning (Phase 11, 16/16)** and **LLM fine-tuning (Phase 12, 25/26)**. Only MLOps (Phase 15) has not been started, so Unit 4 is covered in substance while S10 stays open on that one line.

Two cautions belong next to the RL and LLM checkmarks rather than in a footnote. **11.2.10 measured that removing RL costs the pipeline nothing in executability** - its only gain over a plain DFS is +0.026 semantic from loop marks, while the learned order costs 0.024-0.050 edge F1 - so the syllabus requirement is demonstrably *implemented and evaluated*, which is what it asks, not *load-bearing*. **12.2.7 found fine-tuning statistically tied with few-shot prompting on pass@1** (paired bootstrap CI [-0.0988, 0.0062]); LoRA earns its place on contract compliance, executability and a 2.8x latency saving rather than on correctness. Both are honest negative results and are recorded as such in the rows above.

---

## Headline Success Criteria

| # | Criterion | Target | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :---: |
| S1 | Diagram-type classification accuracy (held-out scribes) | ≥ 92% | ✅ **0.9871 accuracy** (std 0.0009; worst repeat 0.9858) over 1,340 real pages under repeated 5-fold scribe-grouped CV. `src/classify/s1.py` replaces the old handcrafted logistic-regression baseline with the selected CLIP ViT-B/32 embedding (128 PCA components) plus one-vs-one RBF SVM; `reports/s1_heldout_scribes.json` records the three repeat results and the 740 rows / 170 known scribes whose writer identities were actually held out. Audited against the two ways this number could have been hollow, and it survives both. **The stored PCA is fitted on all 1,340 rows**, which is the leak `src/embed/reduce.py` warns about in its own docstring; refitting the projection inside each fold from the raw 512 dimensions costs **0.0053** (0.9818 accuracy, 0.9294 macro F1) and still clears the bar. **The corpus is also perfectly source-confounded** - hdbpmn is 600/600 flowchart, sketch2code 600/600 wireframe, so provenance fixes the label for 1,200 of 1,340 rows and the 600 unknown-scribe rows are exactly the wireframe class - but on the 140 chaos rows, where the source is held constant and the confound cannot operate, the same model scores **0.9643 against a 0.3571 majority baseline** under grouped CV. The classifier is reading diagram structure, not capture conditions. |
| S2 | Component detection mAP@0.5 | ≥ 0.80 | ✅ **0.9107 mAP@0.5** over 308 held-out validation pages / 37,837 predictions (target ≥ 0.80); the stricter hand-drawn hdbpmn slice independently reaches **0.8787** **Rebuild note:** the DVC store was empty, so the corpus, splits and weights were rebuilt from source; this rerun reproduces the pipeline closely but not exactly, and the figures here are the reproducible ones rather than the pre-rebuild values the row previously carried; over 128 pages. `src/detect/s2.py` validates 9.1's raw-prediction evaluation artifact and writes `reports/s2_component_detection.json`. |
| S3 | Label OCR character error rate | ≤ 0.15 | ❌ **0.257** CER (`src/ocr/s3.py` -> `reports/s3_label_ocr.json`; exact match **0.5569**, WER 0.3502), down from 9.3.6's 0.6816 and from this criterion's own 0.3354, but **missing the bar by 1.7x**. **Rebuild note:** the DVC store was empty, so the corpus, splits and weights were rebuilt from source; this rerun reproduces the pipeline closely but not exactly, and the figures here are the reproducible ones rather than the pre-rebuild values the row previously carried, which for this row is a real regression: the pre-rebuild run measured 0.1964 CER / 0.6336 exact. **A second and larger scoping problem is now recorded rather than left implicit: `labelcrops.build` is hardcoded to `load_ir(["hdbpmn"])`, so this criterion is trained and evaluated on hdbpmn alone and has never seen a fa_bresler label in any split** - measured directly, S3 scores **1.3% exact (3 of 233)** on automaton node labels, and a TrOCR fine-tune on the text-crop corpus's fa_bresler rows reaches **94.4%** on the same crops (10.1.2). So 0.257 is not a corpus-wide number; correcting the corpus is expected to move it further from the bar before any retraining moves it back. The 9.3 plateau's root cause was never the recogniser: **a fifth of the corpus was cropped in a place that does not contain its own label.** A BPMN event is a small circle with its text written *underneath*; 9.3.1 cropped the circle and asserted the label was 'application received', and the same held for data objects, lane titles and edge labels - so thirteen architectures agreeing within 0.02 in 9.3.6 was a corpus-wide floor, not a shared bottleneck. `src/ocr/labelcrops.py` now finds the writing first (CRAFT detection) and then decides which element owns it. **Three fixes carried 0.3354 to 0.1964.** *Coverage*: 87.4% -> **100%** (`unlocated_labels` 287 -> **0**), so no label is scored as an empty prediction any more and the headline and located-only numbers are finally the same number. *Containers*: `src/ocr/vertical.py` re-runs CRAFT on each container's header strip turned a quarter turn, the orientation the title is actually written in, adding 5,729 boxes - pool 0.7150 -> **0.2648** and Lane 0.5783 -> **0.2914**. *Ownership*: `src/ocr/ownlearn.py` replaces the hand-written geometry cost with a learned ranker over 27 geometry features plus reading confidence, resolved by Hungarian assignment, and it is trained weakly - the label selects which *training* crop to keep and is never consulted for val or test. Selection quality across four EM rounds: hand cost 0.4676 -> **0.4189**, learned pick 0.3177 -> **0.2662**, oracle pick 0.1422 -> **0.1013**. **That oracle is the finding that matters: the crops already in hand can produce a sub-0.15 model, and the entire remaining 0.0964 is selection, not recognition.** What is left is concentrated and named: Participant **0.5472** (115 crops, the last container class whose title the rotated pass still misses), Flow **0.3084** and edges as a kind **0.3067** against nodes' 0.1748, where deciding whether a line belongs to the arrow passing through it or the circle above it is exactly the text-region annotation 9.1.2 established this project has never had. Activity **0.0539** and task **0.0872** are comfortably inside the bar. Round 4 returned only 0.0021 and carried a warning: confidence-vs-CER correlation weakened -0.3086 -> **-0.2419**, because a better reader grows confident on crops it still gets wrong, eroding the ranker's most informative feature - so further EM rounds decay rather than compound, and the next move is to score candidates by per-character likelihood instead. Two dead ends are recorded: the first S3 run drove training loss to 0.0044 with nothing watching and scored 0.4768 by **memorising the 3,197-string label vocabulary** (90% of predictions were a verbatim training label; 0.1545 exact on unseen phrases), which is why the module selects its epoch on a dev split carved from the training writers - and why `dev_writers()` is excluded from label-selected cropping, a contamination that briefly showed a false 0.128; and a hand-rolled morphology text-finder was tried before CRAFT and abandoned after four rounds - its connector filter deleted long cursive words, turning `'receive order by website'` into a 20px crop. **Re-measured after the corpus scoping bug above was actually fixed, and the numbers moved in the direction that row predicted they would not.** `labelcrops.SOURCES` is now `("hdbpmn", "fa_bresler")` rather than hdbpmn alone, and the ownership ranker was refitted on the resulting corpus: **CER 0.257 -> 0.2400 and exact match 0.5569 -> 0.6321** over 2,835 crops, 23 evaluation writers, zero writer overlap. The prediction in this row was that correcting the corpus would move the number *further* from the bar before retraining moved it back; instead one pass did both. **Per source it is now reportable at all, which it was not before**: hdbpmn **0.2412 CER / 0.5823 exact** over 2,205 crops and fa_bresler **0.1980 CER / 0.8063 exact** over 630 - so the 1.3%-exact figure this row quotes for automaton labels is superseded by 80.6%, and the remaining distance to the bar is hdbpmn's. The refitted ranker is also better on its own terms: hand cost **0.3859**, learned pick **0.1890**, oracle pick **0.0737**, against the 0.4189 / 0.2662 / 0.1013 recorded above. **The criterion still fails at 0.2400 against 0.15**, and the oracle says the same thing it said before - 0.0737 is what perfect selection over the crops already in hand would score, so what is left is still selection rather than recognition |
| S4 | HMM semantic-role macro-F1 | ≥ 0.80 | ✅ **0.8003 macro F1** (std 0.0010; worst repeat 0.7989; clears the bar on 6 of 10 seeds) over 993 sequences / 14,056 nodes, `src/parse/s4.py` -> `reports/s4_role_labelling.json`. **The margin is 0.0003 against a seed spread of 0.0010, so this meets the target rather than beating it, and it is recorded that way rather than rounded up.** The gain over 7.3.9's 0.7763 is one bug and one smoothing change, and neither adds information the model did not have. **7.3.7's decoder ran Viterbi straight through the component breaks 7.3.4 excluded from training**: `transitions.counts` treats a break as a new initial state - nothing in the diagram justifies the transition, and `terminal -> start` across two BPMN pools "would have become the matrix's confident lie" - while `decode_sequence` decoded each page as one unbroken chain across those same 2,524 boundaries, in 555 of the 993 sequences. Decoding each connected component as its own chain is worth **+0.0244** (mean over five seeds, minimum +0.0238, insensitive to alpha), and **91% of that gain is `input` and `output` alone**, whose F1 move **0.9629 -> 0.9968** and **0.8305 -> 0.9934**. That is 7.3.4's own finding closing the loop: all 782 `input` and 303 `output` nodes are isolated single-node components whose transition rows are pure smoothing, so the row already said Viterbi "can reach those states only through the emissions and the initial distribution" - per-component decoding is what lets it, instead of forcing them through transitions the matrix says do not exist. `loop-back` is the one state made worse (-0.0132) and is not netted away. Interpolating the 69%-unobserved emission matrix with the product of its three factor marginals adds a further **+0.0013**, its weight chosen by inner 3-fold CV *inside each training fold* so the evaluation rows never select it. The break positions are union-find over `nodes` and `edges`, computed before `derive_states` runs, so no label information enters. **7.3.9's own prescribed fix was refused**: an arrival-edge factor would hand the model the label function for `branch-true`, `branch-false` and `loop-back`, which `derive_states` assigns *from* the arrival edge and sibling rank - it clears the target easily and measures nothing. Two dead ends recorded so they are not retried: second-order transitions are worth **+0.0002** under nested CV (inner folds pick lambda anywhere in 0.0-0.6, which is what noise looks like) and dropping 7.3.11's inert keyword factor is worth **-0.0005**. 7.3.9's **0.8598** against the four roles a human actually annotated still stands beside this and is still the other real number |
| S5 | Graph edit distance to ground-truth IR | ≤ 3 edits (median) | ❌ **val passes at median 3.0; the frozen test split does not, at 22.5 - and the honest reading is the second one.** Three defects were found and fixed this round, each of them a finished component that had never been connected. (1) **The assembled IR carried no text because no stage ever cropped a node and read it.** `nodetext` composes CRAFT + ownership + S3 and labels *nothing* on fa_bresler: CRAFT finds 0.69 text regions per page against 4.9 labelled states, and S3 answers the few it finds with BPMN vocabulary - `'receive order typ choosen'` on a page whose states are `q0 q1 q2`. The cause is upstream of all of it: **`labelcrops.build` is hardcoded to `load_ir(["hdbpmn"])`, so the corpus S3 trains *and evaluates* on contains no fa_bresler in any split** - S3 scores **1.3% exact** (3 of 233) on automaton labels, not badly but blindly. `src/assemble/statelabels.py` crops the node box directly (the label is *inside* the circle, so there is no detection problem to solve) and reads it with TrOCR fine-tuned on the text-crop corpus's 2,646 fa_bresler rows, which do exist: **94.4% exact against S3's 1.3%**. (2) **10.1.5 was built, measured and never run or wired.** `irdiff._edge_keys` keys a directed edge as an ordered pair and an undirected one as a sorted pair, so an unoriented prediction can never match a truth edge not already in sorted order - **52.6% of hdbpmn's and 23.5% of fa_bresler's, 1,194 of 2,499 edges unmatchable by construction**. Wiring it is worth median 9.0 → 7.0 but only **+1 page**, because failing pages fail by margins orientation cannot close; the oracle bound is the same 7.0, so this is not a fitting shortfall. (3) **Phantom self-loops**: the tracer attaches both ends of a stub to the nearest box, and when that is the same box twice the result is a loop nobody drew - **80 of fa_bresler's 147 false edges**. They separate cleanly from real loops by the longest supporting polyline (median **0.166** of the page diagonal against **0.035**), and `MIN_LOOP_LENGTH = 0.072` removes all 80 for 4 true loops. **It is not fa_bresler tuning: it more than halves hdbpmn's edge inserts too, 740 → 343.** Together, val goes **9.0 → 3.0 median, 128 → 159 of 308 pages at ≤ 3 (41.6% → 51.6%)**. **The mark stays ❌ for two reasons that matter more than the val number.** First, `MIN_LOOP_LENGTH` is fitted on val, which is this module's declared tuning split, so val is not a held-out claim. Second and decisively, **val contains 132 flowchartseg pages that pass for free and the test split contains none**: test is 48 fa_bresler + 114 hdbpmn, and it measures **median 22.5, 22.8% at ≤ 3**. Per source the work is real where it was done - **fa_bresler val 10.0 → 2.0 (8.3% → 72.9%) and test 2.0 at 77.1%, with 2 text edits across 48 pages, so the recogniser and the loop filter both generalise to unseen writers** - and untouched where it was not: **hdbpmn 29.0 val / 29.5 test, 0% at ≤ 3 on both**, carrying 1,620 text edits and 1,251 edge deletes on test. **hdbpmn is now the entire criterion**, and its text mass is the same unfixed omission (nothing crops its task boxes and reads them, though S3 *is* trained on them) while its edge mass is recall on photographs, which 10.1.3's own perfect-ink ceiling caps at 0.7239. **One available gain was refused**: flowchartseg's 8 remaining failures are pure `edge_insert` against a truth carrying **zero** edges, but those pages are flowcharts that visibly have arrows and the annotation simply does not record them - suppressing the traces would have bought +8 pages by deleting probably-correct findings, so it was not done. `reports/s5_graph_ged.json` (val), `reports/s5_graph_ged_test.json` (test) **Re-measured after the tracer was replaced, and the criterion's shape has changed.** 10.1.3's ink-following walk was not under-tuned, it was the wrong method: its own measured ceilings - edge recall 0.7239, precision 0.6386 - put hdbpmn's median GED floor at **12.63** against a target of 3, so the bar sat below the ceiling and no fitting could reach it. `src/detect/arrows.py` replaces it with the published approach (Arrow R-CNN, Schäfer et al. IJDAR 2021, which reports scanned-flowchart diagram recognition rising **37.7% -> 78.6%** where 37.7% is the heuristic generation this tracer belonged to): an arrow is one object class with two keypoints, `kpt_shape [2, 3]`, on ultralytics' existing `PoseTrainer`. Nothing had to be annotated - **489 of 489 hdbpmn edges already carry a polyline**, so `polyline[0]` is the tail and `polyline[-1]` the head - and 993 pages / 14,417 arrows trained in **496 s** to **box mAP@0.5 0.8141, pose mAP@0.5 0.8527**, against the old geometric `arrowhead` class's 0.4318. **On the held-out test split: overall median GED 22.5 -> 16.0, pass share 22.8% -> 26.5%, and fa_bresler 2.0 -> 1.5 median with 77.1% -> 89.6% of pages inside 3 edits.** hdbpmn 28.5 -> 23.5 with its **edge deletes cut 1,251 -> 522**. Val holds at median 3.0 with pass share 51.6% -> 52.6% and mean 11.727 -> 9.896. Two measurements decided the design before a GPU hour was spent: the snapping rule's ceiling with ground-truth keypoints was **0.2128** on hdbpmn until BPMN pools were excluded from the candidates - distance-to-box is zero anywhere inside a box, so a pool spanning the page wins every arrow - and **0.9512** once they were, which is `CONTAINER_AREA`. **It still fails at 16.0 against 3, and what is left is no longer edges.** hdbpmn's dominant cost is now **1,418 `sub_text`** against 522 deletes and 624 inserts, so S5 has become the same problem as S3: reading handwriting off a photograph. Two honest costs are recorded rather than smoothed: edge *inserts* rose (325 -> 624 on test), which is the detector finding arrows nobody drew - self-loops are over-predicted 70 against 51 truth, and keypoint separation does **not** separate the false ones (real median 0.450 of a node side, phantom 0.607, distributions overlapping), so no threshold was added; and the stage is gated to hdbpmn and fa_bresler because **flowchartseg's truth records zero edges** - running it there turned 54 inserts into 1,250 and its median from 0.0 to 9.0, which measures the annotation rather than the detector. `src/assemble/pagetext.py` also moved hdbpmn text 27.0 -> 25.0 on val by reading BPMN labels through 9.3's ownership rules instead of cropping the node box (0.373 -> 0.451 exact on 51 labels) |
| S6 | Generated code executability | ≥ 85% | ✅ **99.4%** of the 162 LoRA test programs run to completion in 11.2.9's sandbox (`reports/llm_quality.md`, 12.3.2), against a zero-shot 40.7% and few-shot 93.8% on the same denominator - unparseable replies count as failures everywhere. 99.4% also parse (12.3.1). **Scope: this is code generated from the IR, not from a photograph** - the vision half reaches the model through Phase 10's assembled IR, whose own fidelity is S5, and no number here is an end-to-end claim. |
| S7 | Functional correctness (pass@1) | ≥ 70% | ✅ **70.37%** pass@1 over 162 test diagrams (`experiments/llm/quality/done.json`, re-scored from scratch 2026-09-20 with the previous artefacts moved aside; reproduces 0.7037 exactly), against the **≥ 70%** target - fa_bresler 66.67% (48), hdbpmn 71.93% (114). **This row previously read 20.4% and that number was a measurement bug, not a model.** 12.3.3 enumerates the decision tree to depth 12, up to 4,096 paths each carrying a full node trace, and the behaviour signature is returned through the sandbox's stdout - which keeps only the **first 1 MiB**. A wide flowchart's signature ran past the cap, came back as half a JSON object and raised `JSONDecodeError` at char 1048564, so the largest diagrams were scored as failures for transport reasons. `20ff03f` gave the candidate program a `/dev/null` sink, kept the real stream for the driver's one-line result and widened `max_output_bytes` - **deliberately widening the transport rather than capping the path set, which would have quietly changed what is scored**. **Three things are recorded against this pass rather than omitted.** (1) The margin is **0.37 points**; a single diagram either way moves it. (2) The model is still **below 12.1.6's emitter, which scores 78.4% under the identical tests** - it reproduces the transcription faithfully rather than improving on it, and on fa_bresler the gap is wide (66.67% against the reference's 91.67%). (3) **`src/llm/pipeline.py` keeps its own 12.3.3 row open under a stricter self-imposed bar** - it substitutes "beat the reference's 78.4%" for the plan's 70% gate on the grounds that the gate "sat above the reference ceiling" - so the phase row and this criterion disagree by design and both are stated. One claim in that module's own text is **wrong and should not be repeated**: it says the emitter "collapses `a,b` into one trigger", and it does not - measured directly, a ground-truth edge labelled `a,b` is emitted as two transitions. Failure reasons: `missing_operation` 16, `verdicts_differ` 15, `order_violation` 7, `crash_or_nontermination` 4, `exclusive_branches_both_ran` 3 |
| S8 | End-to-end latency | < 10 s | ✅ **Measured end-to-end and it fits: median 2.710 s, p90 3.663 s, max 9.673 s over the 25 golden pages, 25/25 inside the 10 s budget** (`reports/p13_cache.json`, 13.6/13.7). Photograph to code, all eight stages, on one card. **Scope stated plainly: this is with 12.1.6's emitter answering, not a served model.** 12.2.9 measures llama.cpp Q4_K_M at p50 6.678 s / p90 10.872 s, so a served arm would be **p50 ~9.4 s - still inside - and p90 ~14.5 s, outside**. Both arms are quoted rather than the flattering one. **The cost is assembly, not the detector or the model**: assemble 2.666 s median against detect 0.0362 s, and every other stage under a millisecond. Two findings behind that number, one of them a correction. It was **5.545 s median until `statelabels._model` was given an `lru_cache`** - `from_pretrained` was reading a 1.3 GB checkpoint on every page for every stage, fifty loads for a 25-page run. But the claim that loading was *essentially all* of it was wrong: after caching, assemble is still 2.666 s, so the load was roughly half and the remaining half is real OCR - two TrOCR decodes per page at beam 4, which is what took the generated state machines from 9 of 9 vacuous to 0 of 8. The `max` is the one number that did not improve (9.343 -> 9.673 s): it is the first page, which still pays the one-time load, and it leaves **0.33 s of headroom**, so the budget is met but not comfortably. 13.6's warm cache takes a re-run to **0.000 s with 150 of 150 lookups hitting** |
| S9 | Live webcam demo succeeds on a first-try messy sketch | Yes | ❌ not reached (Phase 16) |
| S10 | All four syllabus units demonstrably covered | Yes | ❌ Units 1-3 are covered (Phases 5-9, all ✅), and Unit 4 now has four of five: clustering (Phase 8), CNN (Phase 9), RL (Phase 11, 16/16) and LLM fine-tuning (Phase 12, 25/26). **MLOps (Phase 15) is the single remaining line** and has not been started, so this criterion stays open on it alone. |
